Figure 17.
Proveable Uniqueness Theorems

We can show the following properties of the interaction between subtyping and casts axiomatically:

Lemma B.1.

The following hold:

(1)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N:B^{\prime}
.

(2)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle N:A^{\prime}
.

(3)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle P\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle Q:c
.

(4)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle P\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle Q:c
.

Proof.

We have

\frac{\frac{\frac{\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}M\sqsubseteq N:A}{\vdash_{d\sigma}M\sqsubseteq\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N:A\sqsubseteq B}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right = ({ValUpR})}}{\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}M\sqsubseteq\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{right = (\text{Subtyping})}}{\vdash_{d\sigma}\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N:B^{\prime}}\quad\textsc{right = ({ValUpL})}

Dual to the above.

We have

\frac{\frac{\frac{\vdash_{\sigma_{1}}P\sqsubseteq Q:c}{\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}P\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle Q:c}\hskip 8.19447pt\textsc{right = ({EffUpR})}}{\vdash_{d_{\sigma}^{\prime}}P\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle Q:c}\quad\textsc{right = (\text{Subtyping})}}{\vdash_{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle P\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle Q:c}\quad\textsc{right = ({EffUpL})}

Dual to the above.
∎

Appendix C
Operational Semantics

\par\frac{\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\phi)E^{\prime}\#\varepsilon}{E[{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{\prime}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}]\\
\mathrel{\mapsto}E[\phi(\varepsilon)[V/x][(\lambda y.{\texttt{handle}\,\,(E^{\prime}[y])\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}})/k]]}\par\frac{}{E[{\texttt{handle}\,\,V\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[N[V/x]]}\quad\textsc{HandleVal}\par\frac{}{E[(\lambda x.M)V]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[M[V/x]]}\quad\textsc{Lam}\par\frac{}{E[\texttt{let}\,\,x=V\,\texttt{in}\,\,M]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[M[V/x]]}\quad\textsc{Let}\par\frac{}{E[\mho]\mathrel{\mapsto}\mho}\quad\textsc{Err}\par\par\frac{}{E[\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{true}\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[N_{t}]}\quad\textsc{IfTrue}\par\par\frac{}{E[\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{false}\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[N_{f}]}\quad\textsc{IfFalse}\par\frac{}{E[\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle{V}]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[V]}\quad\textsc{EffUpDnCastVal}\par\par\frac{}{E[\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle{V}]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[V]}\quad\textsc{EffDnCastVal}\par\par\par\par\frac{\varepsilon\in\sigma^{\prime}E^{\prime}\#\varepsilon}{E[\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{\prime}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)]]\mathrel{\mapsto}\\
E[\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V)\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{\prime}[x]]}\quad\textsc{EffUpCast}\par\frac{\varepsilon\in\sigma E^{\prime}\#\varepsilon}{E[\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{\prime}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)]]\mathrel{\mapsto}\\
E[\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle V)\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{\prime}[x]]}\quad\textsc{GoodEffDnCast}\par\frac{\varepsilon\notin\sigma E^{\prime}\#\varepsilon}{E[\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{{?}}\rangle E^{\prime}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)]]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[\mho]}\quad\textsc{BadEffDnCast}\par\frac{}{E[\updownarrow\texttt{bool}M]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[M]}\quad\textsc{BoolUpDnCast}\par\par\par\par\par\par\frac{}{E[(\langle{(A^{\prime}\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A\to_{\sigma}B)}\rangle V_{f})\,V]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle(V_{f}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle V)]}\quad\textsc{FunUpCast}\par\frac{}{E[(\langle{(A\to_{\sigma}B)}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{(A^{\prime}\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B^{\prime})}\rangle V_{f})\,V]\mathrel{\mapsto}E[\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(V_{f}\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V)]}\quad\textsc{FunDnCast}\par

Figure 18.
Full Operational Semantics

\Delta::=\bullet:({\sigma}\,!\,{A})\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\Delta}\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A\to_{\sigma}B}\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{\sigma}{N}:{A}}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\Delta}\vdash_{\sigma}{E\,N}:{B}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vdash_{\sigma}{V}:{A\to_{\sigma}B}\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\bullet:({\sigma_{i}}\,!\,{C})}\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\bullet:({\sigma_{i}}\,!\,{C})}\vdash_{\sigma}{V\,E}:{B}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{\texttt{bool}}\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vdash_{\sigma}{N_{t}}{B}\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vdash_{\sigma}{N_{f}}{B}}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{\texttt{if}\,\,E\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}}:{B}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\Delta}\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}\varepsilon@A\leadsto B\in\sigma}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\Delta}\vdash_{\sigma}{\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(E)}:{B}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}\quad\quad{\Sigma}\,\,|\,\,{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash_{\tau}{N}:{B}\quad\quad(\forall(\varepsilon:A_{\varepsilon}\leadsto B_{\varepsilon})\in\sigma.~(\varepsilon\not\in\textrm{dom}(\phi)\wedge(\varepsilon:A_{\varepsilon}\leadsto B_{\varepsilon})\in\tau)\quad\quad\quad\vee({\Sigma}\,\,|\,\,{\Gamma,x:A_{\varepsilon},k:B_{\varepsilon}\mathrel{\to_{\tau}}B}\vdash_{\tau}{\phi(\varepsilon)}:{B}))}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vdash_{\tau}{{\texttt{handle}\,\,E\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}}:{B}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}{\Sigma}\,\,|\,\,{\Gamma,x:A}\vdash_{\sigma}{N}:{B}}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{\texttt{let}\,\,x=E\,\texttt{in}\,\,N}:{B}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A^{\prime}}\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vdash A^{\prime}\leq A}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\Delta}{\sigma^{\prime}}:{E}{A}\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vdash\sigma^{\prime}\leq\sigma}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\Delta}\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}\Sigma\vdash A\sqsubseteq B}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle E}:{B}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{B}\Sigma\vdash A\sqsubseteq B}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle E}:{A}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{A}\Sigma\vdash\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma^{\prime}}{\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E}:{A}}\par\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma^{\prime}}{E}:{A}\Sigma\vdash\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E}:{A}}\par

Figure 19.
Typing Rules for Evaluation Contexts

An evaluation context
E_{\#\varepsilon}
is one in which none of
the handler clauses in the spine of the context handles
\varepsilon
.

\par\frac{}{\varepsilon\#\bullet}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle E)}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle E)}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E\varepsilon\notin\sigma\varepsilon\notin\sigma^{\prime}}{\varepsilon\#(\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E)}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E\varepsilon\notin\sigma^{\prime}}{\varepsilon\#(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E)}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E\varepsilon^{\prime}\text{ any effect}}{\varepsilon\#(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(E))}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E\wedge\varepsilon\not\in\textrm{dom}(\phi)}{\varepsilon\#({\texttt{handle}\,\,E\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}})}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(E\,M)}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(V\,E)}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\texttt{if}\,\,E\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\})}\par\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\texttt{let}\,\,x=E\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)}\par

Figure 20.
Apartness of Effect from an Evaluation Context

C.1.
Operational Semantics from First Principles

Now we show that every operational reduction is justified by our
inequational theory.

Lemma C.1 (Effect Casts are Handlers).

Let
\sigma\sqsubseteq\tau
where
\sigma
is a concrete effect set.

Then the upcast
\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle
is equivalent to a handler in that for any
M:{\sigma}\,!\,{A}
:

\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\equiv{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}\}}

where for each
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\sigma)

x,k\vdash\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}(\varepsilon)=k(\langle{B_{\sigma}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{\tau}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{A_{\tau}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{\sigma}}\rangle))

where
\varepsilon@A_{\sigma}\leadsto B_{\sigma}\in\sigma
and

\varepsilon@A_{\tau}\leadsto B_{\tau}\in\tau
.

Similarly, the downcast
\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle
is equivalent to a
handler in that for any
N:{\tau}\,!\,{A}
:

\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle M\equiv{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}\}}

where for each
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\tau)
, if
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
, then

x,k\vdash\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}(\varepsilon)=k(\langle{B_{\tau}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{\sigma}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{A_{\sigma}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{\tau}}\rangle))

and if
\varepsilon\not\in\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
, then

\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}(\varepsilon)=\mho

Proof.

First for the upcast case

•

We want to show

\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}\}}

By UpL, it is sufficient to show

M\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}\}}

But by the handler
\eta
rule, this is equivalent to showing

{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\sigma}\}}\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}\}}

where
\textrm{dom}(\phi_{\sigma})=\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
and
\phi_{\sigma}(\varepsilon)=k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))
.
Then by congruence, we need to show that for each
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
,

k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\sqsubseteq k(\langle{B_{\sigma}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{\tau}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{A_{\sigma}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{)}\rangle{A_{\tau}}x)

which follows from UpR/DnR and congruence rules

•

We want to show

{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}\}}\sqsubseteq\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M

By handler
\eta
it is sufficient to show

{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}\}}\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\tau}\}}

where
\textrm{dom}(\phi_{\tau})=\textrm{dom}(\tau)
and
\phi_{\tau}(\varepsilon)=k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))
.
Then
M\sqsubseteq{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M}
by UpR and so
by congruence we need only to show for each
\varepsilon\in\sigma

that

\phi_{\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle}(\varepsilon)\sqsubseteq\phi_{\tau}(\varepsilon)

which follows by a similar argument to the previous case.

Next, the downcast cases.

•

We want to show

{\texttt{handle}\,\,N\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}\}}\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle N

By DnR, it is sufficient to show

{\texttt{handle}\,\,N\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}\}}\sqsubseteq N

By handler
\eta
this is equivalent to showign

{\texttt{handle}\,\,N\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}\}}\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,N\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\tau}\}}

That is, for any
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\tau)
that

\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}(\varepsilon)\sqsubseteq\phi_{\tau}(\varepsilon)

There are two cases

(1)

If
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
, then we need to show

k(\langle{B_{\tau}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{\sigma}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{A_{\tau}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{\sigma}}\rangle x))\sqsubseteq k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))

which follows by congruence and DnL/UpL rules.

(2)

If
\varepsilon\notin\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
, then we need to show

\mho\sqsubseteq k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))

which is immediate.

•

We want to show

\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle N\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,N\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}\}}

By handler
\eta
this is equivalent to showing

{\texttt{handle}\,\,(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle N)\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\sigma}\}}\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,N\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.x\,\,|\,\,\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}\}}

By congruence and DnL this reduces to showing for each
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
that

\phi_{\sigma}(\varepsilon)\sqsubseteq\phi_{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle}(\varepsilon)

since
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\sigma)
, these are each of the form:

k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\sqsubseteq k(\langle{B_{\tau}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{\sigma}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{A_{\tau}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{\sigma}}\rangle x))

which follows by congruence and DnR/UpR rules.

∎

Lemma C.2 (Derivation of Function Casts).

\langle{A^{\prime}\mathrel{\to_{\tau}}B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A\mathrel{\to_{\sigma}}B}\rangle f\equiv\lambda x.\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle(f(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle x))

And similarly,

\langle{A\mathrel{\to_{\sigma}}B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}\mathrel{\to_{\tau}}B^{\prime}}\rangle f\equiv\lambda x.\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\tau}\rangle(f(\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x))

Proof.

We show the upcast cases, the downcast cases are precisely dual.

(1)

We want to show

\langle{A^{\prime}\mathrel{\to_{\tau}}B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A\mathrel{\to_{\sigma}}B}\rangle f\sqsubseteq\lambda x.\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle(f(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle x))

By UpL, it is sufficient to show

f\sqsubseteq\lambda x.\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle(f(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle x))

By
\eta
equivalence for functions it is sufficient to show

\lambda x.fx\sqsubseteq\lambda x.\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle(f(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle x))

Which follows by congruence rules and UpR/DnR rules.

(2)

We want to show

\lambda x.\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle(f(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle x))\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}\mathrel{\to_{\tau}}B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A\mathrel{\to_{\sigma}}B}\rangle f

By function
\eta
it is sufficient to show

\lambda x.\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle(f(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle x))\sqsubseteq\lambda y.(\langle{A^{\prime}\mathrel{\to_{\tau}}B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A\mathrel{\to_{\sigma}}B}\rangle f)y

Which follows by congruence and UpL/DnL/UpR rules.

∎

Lemma C.3.

If
x,k\vdash\phi(\varepsilon)=k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))
, then

{\texttt{handle}\,\,\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,o=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,{\texttt{handle}\,\,o\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}

Proof.

\displaystyle{\texttt{handle}\,\,\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}

\displaystyle\equiv{\texttt{handle}\,\,(\texttt{let}\,\,o=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,o\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}

\displaystyle\equiv(\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,o\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}})(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,o=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,{\texttt{handle}\,\,o\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}

∎

This lemma is useful for the cast cases of the following, as it
reduces to showing the cast is equivalent to one whose
\varepsilon
case
is just a re-raise.

Lemma C.4.

If
E\#\varepsilon
, then

E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[y]

Proof.

By induction on
\varepsilon\#E

•

\frac{}{\varepsilon\#\bullet}

\displaystyle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,y

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle E)}

\displaystyle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,z=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z])\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=E[z]\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{E[z]}

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle E)}

Similar to previous.

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(E))}

\displaystyle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(x)])

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}((\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z]))

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z]\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(())

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E\varepsilon\not\in\textrm{dom}(\phi)}{\varepsilon\#({\texttt{handle}\,\,E\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}})}

Define
\psi
to be the extension of
\phi
with the case

\psi(\varepsilon)=k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))
.

\displaystyle{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}

\displaystyle\equiv{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\psi\}}

\displaystyle\equiv{\texttt{handle}\,\,(\texttt{let}\,\,z=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z])\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\psi\}}

\displaystyle\equiv(\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\psi\}})(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))

\displaystyle\equiv(\texttt{let}\,\,o=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\psi\}})

\displaystyle\equiv(\texttt{let}\,\,o=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}})

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(E\,M)}

\displaystyle(E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)])M

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,f=E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,f\,y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,f=(\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z])\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,f\,y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,f=E[z]\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,f\,y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,(E[z])\,M

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(V\,E)}

\displaystyle(V\,E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)])

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,f=V\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\,\texttt{in}\,\,f\,y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,f=V\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z])\,\texttt{in}\,\,f\,y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z])\,\texttt{in}\,\,V\,y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=(E[z])\,\texttt{in}\,\,V\,y

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,V\,(E[z])

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\texttt{if}\,\,E\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\})}

\displaystyle\texttt{if}\,\,E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=(E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)])\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,y\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z])\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,y\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=(E[z])\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,y\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,E[z]\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}

•

\frac{\varepsilon\#E}{\varepsilon\#(\texttt{let}\,\,x=E\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)}

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\,\texttt{in}\,\,N

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,y=\texttt{let}\,\,z=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z]\,\texttt{in}\,\,N

\displaystyle\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,z=(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=E[z]\,\texttt{in}\,\,N

∎

Theorem C.5 (Soundness of Operational Semantics).

If
M\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}M^{\prime}
then
M\equiv M^{\prime}
is derivable in the
inequational theory.

Proof.

(1)

The value handle, boolean/function
\beta
reductions and error reduction are immediate by axioms.

(2)

\frac{E\#\varepsilon}{{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}\equiv\phi(\varepsilon)[V/x,\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}/k]}

(Lemma
C.4
)

\displaystyle{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}

\displaystyle\equiv{\texttt{handle}\,\,(\texttt{let}\,\,z=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)\,\texttt{in}\,\,E[z])\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}

\displaystyle\equiv\phi(\varepsilon)[V/x,\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}/k]

(3)

\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle{V}\equiv V

By the following:

(Lemma

\displaystyle\langle{\tau}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle{V}

(Handle
\beta

\displaystyle\equiv V

is similar to the

Theorem C.6 (Adequacy).

If
\cdot\vdash_{\emptyset}M\equiv M^{\prime}:\texttt{bool}
is derivable in the equational theory
than for any
R\in\{\texttt{true},\texttt{false},\mho\}

M\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}R\iff M^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}R

Corollary C.7 (Consistency).

\texttt{true}\equiv\texttt{false}
is not derivable.

Theorem C.8 (Graduality).

If
\cdot\vdash_{\emptyset}M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:\texttt{bool}

Then for any
R\in\{\texttt{true},\texttt{false}\}
,

M\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}R\Rightarrow M^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}R

and for any
R^{\prime}\in\{\texttt{true},\texttt{false},\mho\}
,

M^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}R^{\prime}\implies M\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}R^{\prime}

Appendix D
Elaboration

Lemma D.1.

If
A\lesssim B
then there exist types
A_{h},D_{h},D_{l},B_{l}
with

(1)

c_{l}:A\sqsubseteq D_{l}
and
c_{h}:A_{h}\sqsubseteq D_{h}
satisfying
c_{l}\leq c_{h}

(2)

d_{l}:B_{l}\sqsubseteq D_{l}
and
d_{h}:B\sqsubseteq D_{h}
satisfying
d_{l}\leq d_{h}

(3)

e_{l}:D_{l}\sqsubseteq D
and
e_{h}:D_{h}\sqsubseteq D
with
e_{l}\leq e_{h}
where
D=\lceil A\rceil=\lceil B\rceil
.

Proof.

By induction on the proof of
A\lesssim A^{\prime}
.
∎

Then the four different choices of cast are all
equivalent in the inequational theory:

Lemma D.2.

Given
A,A_{h},B,B_{l},D_{l},D_{h},D,c_{l},c_{h},d_{l},d_{h},e_{l},e_{h}
as in the
output of the previous lemma, for any
\Gamma\vdash M:{\sigma}\,!\,{A}
, the following four terms are equivalent at type
B
.

(1)

\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D_{h}}\rangle\langle{D_{h}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{h}}\rangle M

(2)

\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D_{h}}\rangle\langle{D_{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M

(3)

\langle{B_{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D_{l}}\rangle\langle{D_{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M

(4)

\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M

Proof.

(1)

To show (1) is equivalent to (2), it suffices to show

\langle{D_{h}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{h}}\rangle M\equiv\langle{D_{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M

which is an instance of the subtyping/cast rule since
c_{l}\sqsubseteq c_{h}
.

(2)

Similarly to show (2) is equivalent to (3) follows from

d_{l}\leq d_{h}

(3)

Lastly we show (4) is equivalent to (2).
By cast functoriality,

\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M\equiv\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D_{h}}\rangle\langle{D_{h}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D_{l}}\rangle\langle{D_{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M

And by retraction the middle cast
\langle{D_{h}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D_{l}}\rangle

is the identity.

∎

\begin{array}[]{rcl}\text{VAtom}\,{c}&:=&\{(V^{l},V^{r}):\texttt{val}(V^{l})\wedge\texttt{val}(V^{r})\wedge\\
&&\quad(\Sigma\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{\emptyset}{V^{l}}:{c^{l}})\wedge(\Sigma\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{\emptyset}{V^{r}}:{c^{r}})\}\\

&&\quad(\Sigma\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}^{l}}{M^{l}}:{A^{l}})\wedge(\Sigma\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}^{r}}{M^{r}}:{A^{r}})\}\\
\\
\text{ECtxAtom}\,{c}\,{({\sigma^{l}}\,!\,{A^{l}})}\,{({\sigma^{r}}\,!\,{A^{r}})}&:=&\{(x^{l}.M^{l},x^{r}.M^{r}):\\
&&\quad(\Sigma\,\,|\,\,{x^{l}:c^{l}}\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{\sigma^{l}}{M^{l}}:{A^{l}})\wedge(\Sigma\,\,|\,\,{x^{r}:c^{r}}\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{\sigma^{r}}{M^{r}}:{A^{r}})\}\par\end{array}

We begin with a few lemmas that will be useful in our proofs.

D.1.1.
Lemmas

Lemma D.4.

If
(V_{1},V_{2})\in R
, and
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are values of type
A^{l}
and
A^{r}
respectively, then
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{(R,A^{l},A^{r})}
.

Proof.

We will establish the first disjunct in the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket{}
. This follows by assumption.
∎

Lemma D.5.

If
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
, then
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

Proof.

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
, and suppose
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
. Notice that regardless of whether
\sim
is
<
or
>
, we will be able to show the last clause in the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
or
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
. In particular, we can take
k=j
,
V_{1}=V_{1}
, and
V_{2}=V_{2}
, noting that
V_{1}
steps to itself in
0
steps, as does
V_{2}
. Thus, it remains to show that
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are related by
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
or
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
. This is true by assumption.
∎

Lemma D.6.

If
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket
, then
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
D.5
(with
R=\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket
), it suffices to show that
(\sigma_{1}V_{1},\sigma_{1}V_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. This is true by Lemma
D.4
, again with
R=\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.
∎

Lemma D.7 (anti-reduction, one-sided).

Suppose
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}M_{1}^{\prime}
and
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
.

If
(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j-i_{2}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
, then

(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

Similarly, if
(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j-i_{1}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
, then

(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

Proof.

We prove the first statement; the second is analogous (and in fact easier).
The assumption that
(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j-i_{2}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
has four cases:

(1)

M_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-i_{2}+1}}
. In this case,
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}M_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-i_{2}+1}}
, i.e,
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j+1}}
. Thus, we may assert the first disjunct in the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

(2)

There exists
k\leq j-i_{2}
such that
M_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-i_{2}-k}}\mho
, and furthermore
M_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
. In this case, we have that
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}M_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-i_{2}-k}}\mho
, so
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}\mho
. Also,
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}M_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
, so
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
. Thus, we may assert the second disjunct.

(3)

There exists
k\leq j-i_{2}
and
N_{2}
such that
M_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-i_{2}-k}}N_{2}
and
M_{1}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
. In this case we have
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}M_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-i_{2}-k}}N_{2}
, so
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}N_{2}
. Thus, we may assert the third disjunct.

(4)

Similar to previous case.

∎

Lemma D.8 (anti-reduction).

Suppose
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}M_{1}^{\prime}
and
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
, and that

(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j-m}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
, where
m=\min\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
.
Then
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

Proof.

Follows from one-sided anti-reduction (Lemma
D.7
) and downward closure.
∎

Lemma D.9 (forward reduction, one-sided).

Suppose
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}M_{1}^{\prime}
and
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
.

If
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j+i_{2}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
, then

(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

Similarly, if
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j+i_{1}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
, then

(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

Proof.

Follows from determinism of evaluation and a case analysis on the assumption that
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
are related.
∎

Lemma D.10 (forward reduction).

Suppose
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}M_{1}^{\prime}
and
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}M_{2}^{\prime}
, and that

(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j+m}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
, where
m=\max\{i_{1},i_{2}\}
.
Then
(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(R,A^{l},A^{r})
.

Proof.

Follows from one-sided forward reduction (Lemma
D.9
) and downward closure.
∎

Frequently in our proofs we will encounter a situation where we know that two
evaluation contexts are related in the
\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket{}
relation, that is,
substituting related values gives related outputs.
On the other hand, as a cast applied to a value is not necessarily itself a value,
we cannot reason directly about what happens when such semantic values are substituted into
related evaluation contexts. We therefore introduce the following lemma.

Lemma D.11.

Suppose
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
are evaluation contexts that take values to values.
Let
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
be values (not necessarily related) such that

(E_{1}[V_{1}],E_{2}[V_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

Furthermore, let
(E^{l}[x^{l}],E^{r}[x^{r}]\in\mathcal{K}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket{\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}})
.

Then

(E^{l}[E_{1}[V_{1}]],E^{r}[E_{2}[V_{2}]])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

Proof.

We show the proof for
\sim\,=\,>
.

By assumption, we have that there exist values
V_{1}^{\prime}
and
V_{2}^{\prime}
such that

E_{1}[V_{1}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}V_{1}^{\prime}
and
E_{2}[V_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}V_{2}^{\prime}
, for some
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
.

Thus,
E^{l}[E_{1}[V_{1}]]\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}]
and likewise
E^{r}[E_{2}[V_{2}]]\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime}]
.

By one-sided anti-reduction (Lemma
D.7
), it suffices to show that

(E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}],E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j-i_{2}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

By assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
being related, it suffices to show that

(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j-i_{2}}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.

Now by one-sided forward reduction (Lemma
D.9
), it suffices to show

(E_{1}[V_{1}],E_{2}[V_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{(j-i_{2})+i_{2}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

But this is precisely our assumption, so we are finished.

∎

Remark:

The reason why we needed to consider cases on
\sim
separately is that the more “generic”/two-sided
anti-reduction and forward-reduction lemmas involve the
\min
or
\max
of the number of steps
taken by the two terms. These may not be equal, in which case the arithmetic wouldn’t work out.
But this doesn’t mean the above lemma is false.
Conceptually, what is happening is that in the two-sided variants of the lemmas,

\sim
could be either
>
or
<
.
On the other hand, the key here is that
\sim
stays the same throughout the application of
anti-reduction and forward reduction, so we are able to use the more specific, one-sided lemmas.

Lemma D.12 (time-out).

If
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{(i+1)}}
, then
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket R
.
Similarly, if
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{(i+1)}}
, then
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket R
.

Proof.

Suppose
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{(i+1)}}
. Then we may assert the first disjunct in the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket R
to conclude that
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket R
. Likewise, if
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{(i+1)}}
, then we may assert the first disjunct in the defintion of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket R
to conclude that
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket R
.
∎

We present two trivial lemmas about the later modality. We do this to cut down on tedious reasoning about step indices within other proofs.

Lemma D.13.

Let
R
be a monotone step-indexed relation. If
(M_{1},M_{2})\in R_{j}
, then
(M_{1},M_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}R)_{j}
.

Proof.

Suppose
(M_{1},M_{2})\in R_{j}
. If
j=0
, then
(M_{1},M_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}R)_{0}
trivially.

Otherwise, let
j=j^{\prime}+1
. By monotonicity of
R
, we have
(M_{1},M_{2})\in R_{j^{\prime}}
, from which it follows that
(M_{1},M_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}R)_{j}
.
∎

Lemma D.14.

Let
R
be a monotone step-indexed relation, and let
j
be of the form
j=j^{\prime}+1
. If
(M_{1},M_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}R)_{j}
, then
(M_{1},M_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}R)_{j^{\prime}}
.

Proof.

Suppose
(M_{1},M_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}R)_{j}
. Since
j=j^{\prime}+1
, by definition of
{\blacktriangleright}
we must have that
(M_{1},M_{2})\in R_{j^{\prime}}
. By the previous lemma (Lemma
D.13
), we conclude
(M_{1},M_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}R)_{j^{\prime}}
, which is what we needed to show.
∎

Lemma D.15 (Reasoning with “later” when both sides step).

Suppose
M\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}M^{\prime}
and
N\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}N^{\prime}
, and that

(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}{R}
.
Then
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{R}
.

Proof.

First suppose
k=0
. Then by the time-out lemma (Lemma
D.12
),
regardless of whether
\sim
is
<
or
>
, we have
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{0}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket R
.

Now suppose
k\geq 1
. Then by the definition of later, we have that

(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k-1}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{R}
, so by anti-reduction
we have that
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{R}
.
∎

Lemma D.16 (Löb-induction).

Let
P(n)
be a predicate indexed by a natural number
n
.
Suppose for all natural numbers
n
, we have that
({\blacktriangleright}^{m}P)(n)
implies
P(n)
for all
m\geq 1
.
Then
P(n)
is true for all natural numbers
n
.

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
n
. When
n=0
, the assumption says that
({\blacktriangleright}P)(0)
implies
P(0)
(we have taken
m=1
).
So, it suffices to show that
({\blacktriangleright}P)(0)
holds. This is true by the definition of later.

Now let
n\geq 1
be fixed, and suppose
P(n)
is true. We claim that
P(n+1)
is true.
By our assumption, it will suffice to show that
({\blacktriangleright}P)(n+1)
is true. (We have again chosen
m=1
.)
By definition of later, we must show
P(n)
is true. But
P(n)
is true by assumption.
∎

We now introduce a key lemma about evaluation contexts.

Note
: In the below, we omit explicit mention of the types associated to the relations that parameterize
\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket{}
and
\mathcal{R}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket{}
.

Lemma D.17.

If

(1)

(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{S^{\prime}}

(2)

For all
k\leq j
and
(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{S^{\prime}}
, we have
(E_{1}[N_{1}],E_{2}[N_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket S
,

then
(E_{1}[M_{1}],E_{2}[M_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket S
.

Proof.

We prove the lemma for
\sim\,=\,>
; the other case is similar. Based on assumption (1), there are four cases:

(1)

Case
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j+1}}
. We have
E_{2}[M_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{j+1}}
, so we may assert the first disjunct in the definition of
\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket S
to conclude that
(E_{1}[M_{1}],E_{2}[M_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket S
.

(2)

Case
\exists k\leq j
such that
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}\mho
and
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
. We have
E_{2}[M_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k+1}}\mho
. If
k=0
, then we have
E_{2}[M_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{j+1}}
, so we may assert the first disjunct. Otherwise, if
k\geq 1
, then we may take
k^{\prime}=k-1
and observe that
E_{2}[M_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k^{\prime}}}\mho
.

(3)

Case
\exists k\leq j
,
\exists V_{2}
such that
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}N_{2}
and
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
. We have
E_{2}[M_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}E_{2}[N_{2}]
, so we may assert the third disjunct with
k=k
and
N_{2}=E_{2}[N_{2}]
.

(4)

Case
\exists k\leq j,\exists(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{S^{\prime}}
such that
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}N_{2}
and
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}N_{1}
. We have
E_{1}[M_{1}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}E_{1}[N_{1}]
for some
i_{1}
, and
E_{2}[M_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}E_{2}[N_{2}]
. By assumption (2), we have
(E_{1}[N_{1}],E_{2}[N_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket S
.
Thus, we may assert the fourth disjunct with
V_{1}=E_{1}[N_{1}]
and
V_{2}=E_{2}[N_{2}]
.

∎

Lemma D.18 (“Semantic bind”).

Let
c:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}
and
d:B\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
.
Let
E_{1}
and
E_{2}
be evaluation contexts such that

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\bullet:({d_{\sigma}^{\prime l}}\,!\,{A})}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}^{l}}{E_{1}}:{B}
and

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\bullet:({d_{\sigma}^{\prime r}}\,!\,{A^{\prime}})}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}^{r}}{E_{2}}:{B^{\prime}}
.
Suppose

(1)

(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(S^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})
.

(2)

For all
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in S^{\prime}_{k}
, we have
(E_{1}[V_{1}],E_{2}[V_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(S,B,B^{\prime})
.

(3)

For all
k\leq j
and for all
\varepsilon:c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
, if
E_{1}
catches

\varepsilon
or
E_{2}
catches
\varepsilon
, then for all
V^{l},V^{r}\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}

and all evaluation contexts
E^{l}\#\varepsilon
and
E^{r}\#\varepsilon
such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{(S^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})},({d_{\sigma}^{\prime l}}\,!\,{A}),({d_{\sigma}^{\prime r}}\,!\,{A^{\prime}}))
, we have

(E_{1}[E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})]],E_{2}[E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})]])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(S,B,B^{\prime})
.

Then
(E_{1}[M_{1}],E_{2}[M_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(S,B,B^{\prime})
.

Proof.

We use Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
). We assume that if the premises
of the lemma are satisfied “later”, then the conclusion holds later.
We show under this assumption that the lemma holds “now".

We first apply Lemma
D.17
. The first hypothesis is immediate.
Now let
k\leq j
and let
(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket(S^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})
. We need to show that

(E_{1}[N_{1}],E_{2}[N_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(S,B,B^{\prime}).

There are two cases to consider. In the first case,
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
are values and
(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.
Then by assumption (2) with
k=j
, we have
(E_{1}[N_{1}],E_{2}[N_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket(S,B,B^{\prime})
, as needed.

In the second case, there exist

\varepsilon^{\prime}:c^{\prime}\leadsto d^{\prime}\in d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
,
E^{l}\#\varepsilon^{\prime},E^{r}\#\varepsilon^{\prime}
, and
V^{l},V^{r}
such that

(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{j}
, and

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in\\
({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{j}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{(S^{\prime},A,A^{\prime})},({d_{\sigma}^{\prime l}}\,!\,{A}),({d_{\sigma}^{\prime r}}\,!\,{A^{\prime}}))
,
and

N_{1}=E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(V^{l})]
and

N_{2}=E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(V^{r})]
.

Let

N_{1}^{\prime}=E_{1}[N_{1}]=E_{1}[E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(V^{l})]]
and

N_{2}^{\prime}=E_{2}[N_{2}]=E_{2}[E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon^{\prime}(V^{r})]]
.

We need to show that

(N_{1}^{\prime},N_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{(S,B,B^{\prime})}.

We now consider whether one of
E_{1}
or
E_{2}
catches
\varepsilon^{\prime}
, or whether neither catches it.
In the former case, assumption (3) immediately implies the desired result.

Now suppose neither
E_{1}
nor
E_{2}
catches
\varepsilon
. In this case, note that since

\varepsilon^{\prime}\#E^{l}
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}\#E_{1}
, we have
\varepsilon^{\prime}\#E_{1}[E^{l}]
.
Likewise, we have
\varepsilon^{\prime}\#E_{2}[E^{r}]
.
It follows that
N_{1}^{\prime}
and
N_{2}^{\prime}
are stuck terms, i.e., they do not step. Thus, it suffices to show that

(N_{1}^{\prime},N_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{(S,B,B^{\prime})}.

We first claim
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{j}
.
Since
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{j}
,
this follows by Lemma
D.14
.

We now claim that

(x^{l}.(E_{1}[E^{l}[x^{l}]]),x^{r}.(E_{2}[E^{r}[x^{r}]]))\in\\
({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{j}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{(S,B,B^{\prime})},({d_{\sigma}^{l}}\,!\,{B}),({d_{\sigma}^{r}}\,!\,{B^{\prime}})).

To this end, let
k\leq j
and let
(V^{\prime l},V^{\prime r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{k}
. We need to show that

(E_{1}[E^{l}[V^{\prime l}]],E_{2}[E^{r}[V^{\prime r}]])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}{(S,B,B^{\prime})}.

By the Löb induction hypothesis, it suffices to show that the three hypotheses of the lemma hold
later.
We claim that
(E^{l}[V^{\prime l}],E^{r}[V^{\prime r}])\in(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
.
To see this, recall our assumption that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{j}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

Thus, we have that
(E^{l}[V^{\prime l}],E^{r}[V^{\prime r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
,
which is what we needed to show.

∎

We now introduce a few lemmas about precision derivations. We first show how we may “compose” precision derivations:

Lemma D.19 (cut admissibility for precision derivations).

•

If
c:A\sqsubseteq B
and

d:B\sqsubseteq C
then

c\circ d:A\sqsubseteq C
.

•

If
d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
and

d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}
then

d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.

Proof.

We prove these statements simultaneously by induction on
d
and
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
.

•

Case
d=\texttt{bool}
. We have
B=C=\texttt{bool}
, so
c=\texttt{bool}
(the reflexivity derivation). Thus, we may take
c\circ d=\texttt{bool}
.

•

Case
d=d_{i}\to_{d_{\sigma}}d_{o}
. Inspecting the rules in figure
14
,
we see that
B=B_{i}\to_{B_{\sigma}}B_{o}
and
C=C_{i}\to_{C_{\sigma}}C_{o}
. Thus, we must have
A=A_{i}\to_{A_{\sigma}}A_{o}
,
which means that
c=c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}
.

We may take
c\circ d=(c_{i}\circ d_{i})\to_{c_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}}(c_{o}\circ d_{o})
.
By our inductive hypotheses, we have
(1)
c_{i}\circ d_{i}:A_{i}\sqsubseteq C_{i}
,
(2)
c_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}:A_{\sigma}\sqsubseteq C_{\sigma}
, and
(3)
c_{o}\circ d_{o}:A_{o}\sqsubseteq C_{o}
.
Now, using the type precision formation rule for functions, we get that
(c_{i}\circ d_{i})\to_{c_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}}(c_{o}\circ d_{o}):(A_{i}\to_{A_{\sigma}}A_{o}\sqsubseteq C_{i}\to_{C_{\sigma}}C_{o})
.

•

Case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}={?}
. Define
{?}\circ{?}={?}
. Define
\texttt{Inj}(d)\circ{?}=\texttt{Inj}(d)
.
An concrete effect set cannot be composed with
{?}
.

•

Case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}=\texttt{Inj}(d)
. Note that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}={?}
.
We define
d_{\sigma}\circ\texttt{Inj}(d)=\texttt{Inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d)
.

•

Case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}=d_{c}^{\prime}
: Define
(d_{c}\circ d_{c}^{\prime})
by

\varepsilon:c\leadsto d\in(d_{c}\circ d_{c}^{\prime})
if and only if
c=c_{1}\circ c_{2}
and
d=d_{1}\circ d_{2}
with

\varepsilon:c\leadsto_{1}d_{1}\in d_{c}
and
\varepsilon:c\leadsto_{2}d_{2}\in d_{c}^{\prime}
.

∎

Lemma D.20 (reflexivity of composition).

Let
c:A\sqsubseteq B
and
d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
. The following hold.

•

c\circ B=A\circ c=c
.

•

d_{\sigma}\circ\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\circ d_{\sigma}=d_{\sigma}
.

Proof.

Follows from the uniquenes of precision derivations. That is,
c\circ B
,
A\circ c
, and
c

all are all proofs of
A\sqsubseteq B
, hence are equal.
∎

Lemma D.21 (decomposition).

Suppose
\varepsilon@c\leadsto d\in d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
.
Then there exist
c_{1},c_{2}
and
d_{1},d_{2}
such that

\varepsilon@c_{1}\leadsto d_{1}\in d_{\sigma}
and

\varepsilon@c_{2}\leadsto d_{2}\in d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
and

c=c_{1}\circ c_{2}
and
d=d_{1}\circ d_{2}
.

Proof.

By induction on
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
.

•

Case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}={?}
.
If
d_{\sigma}={?}
, then our assumption becomes
\varepsilon@c\leadsto d\in{?}\circ{?}={?}
.
By definition of membership in
{?}
, this means that
\varepsilon@c^{r}\leadsto d^{r}\in\Sigma
.

We may take
c_{1}=c
and take
c_{2}
to be the reflexivity derivation for
c^{r}\sqsubseteq c^{r}
.
Likewise, we take
d_{1}=d
and
d_{2}
to be the relfexivity derivation for
d^{r}\sqsubseteq d^{r}
.
Note that
\varepsilon@c_{2}\leadsto d_{2}\in{?}
, because
c_{2}^{r}=c^{r}
and
d_{2}^{r}=d^{r}
,
and we know
\varepsilon@c^{r}\leadsto d^{r}\in\Sigma
.
We also have that

c=c_{1}\circ c_{2}
and
d=d_{1}\circ d_{2}
, using Lemma
D.20
.

If
d_{\sigma}=\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma})
, then our assumption becomes

\varepsilon@c\leadsto d\in\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma})
. By definition of membership in
\texttt{Inj}({},{})
,
we have that
\varepsilon@c\leadsto d\in d_{\sigma}
.
We may again take
c_{1}=c
and
c_{2}
to be the reflexivity derivation for
c^{r}\sqsubseteq c^{r}
,
and likewise for
d_{1}
and
d_{2}
. The same reasoning as above applies.

•

Case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}=\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma})
.
By definition of composition, our assumption becomes

\varepsilon@c\leadsto d\in(d_{\sigma}\circ\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}))=\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma})
.

By the induction hypothesis, there are
c_{1},c_{2}
and
d_{1},d_{2}
such that

\varepsilon@c_{1}\leadsto d_{1}\in d_{\sigma}
and

\varepsilon@c_{2}\leadsto d_{2}\in d_{\sigma}
and

c=c_{1}\circ c_{2}
and
d=d_{1}\circ d_{2}
.
By definition of membership in
\texttt{Inj}({},{})
, we have

\varepsilon@c_{2}\leadsto d_{2}\in\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma})=d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
.

•

Case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}=d_{c}^{\prime}
(concrete effect set).
Similar to previous case.

∎

D.1.2.
Congruence Rules

With these lemmas, we can prove the soundness of the term precision congruence rules.
The proofs are by induction on the term precision derivation.

Lemma D.22 (Congruence for Booleans).

Proof.

We need to show that
\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}\llbracket\texttt{true}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket\texttt{true}\rrbracket\in\texttt{bool}
, and likewise for
false
(we will show this for
true
only; the reasoning for
false
is exactly the same.)

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show

\left(\texttt{true}[\gamma_{1}],\texttt{true}[\gamma_{2}]\right)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket},

i.e.,

\left(\texttt{true},\texttt{true}\right)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}.

By Lemma
D.6
, it suffices to show that
(\texttt{true},\texttt{true})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket
. This is true according to the definition of the logical relation.

∎

Lemma D.23 (Congruence for Variables).

Proof.

We need to show that
\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}:c,\Gamma^{\prime\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}\in c
.

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
, and let
\widehat{\Gamma}^{\sqsubseteq}=\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}:c,\Gamma^{\prime\sqsubseteq}
. Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\widehat{\Gamma}^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show

\left(x_{1}[\gamma_{1}],x_{2}[\gamma_{2}]\right)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By Lemma
D.6
, it suffices to show that
(\gamma_{1}(x_{1}),\gamma_{2}(x_{2}))\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket c\rrbracket
. But this follows from the fact that
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\widehat{\Gamma}^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. In particular, by the definition of the logical relation, since
(x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}:c)\in\widehat{\Gamma}^{\sqsubseteq}
, we have
(\gamma_{1}(x_{1}),\gamma_{2}(x_{2}))\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.
∎

Lemma D.24 (Congruence for Lambdas).

Proof.

Suppose
\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x\sqsubseteq y:c\vDash_{d_{\sigma^{\prime}}}{M}\sqsubseteq{N}\in d
.
We need to show that
\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}{\lambda x.M}\sqsubseteq{\lambda y.N}\in c\to_{d_{\sigma^{\prime}}}d
.

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show

\left((\lambda x.M)[\gamma_{1}],(\lambda y.N)[\gamma_{2}]\right)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\to_{d_{\sigma^{\prime}}}d\rrbracket}.

Let
V_{1}=\lambda x.{M}[\gamma_{1}]
and
V_{2}=\lambda y.{N}[\gamma_{2}]
.
By Lemma
D.6
, it will suffice to show that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket c\to_{d_{\sigma^{\prime}}}d\rrbracket
. To this end, let
k\leq i
and let
(V_{i1},V_{i2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\rrbracket
. We will show that
(V_{1}\,V_{i1},V_{2}\,V_{i2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma^{\prime}}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}
.

Let
M^{\prime}=({M}[\gamma_{1}])(V_{i1}/x)
and let
N^{\prime}=({N}[\gamma_{2}])(V_{i2}/y)
. Note that
(V_{1}\,V_{i1})\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}M^{\prime}
, and similarly
(V_{2}\,V_{i2})\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}N^{\prime}
. Thus, if
k=0
, then by the Time-out Lemma (Lemma
D.12
), we conclude that
(V_{1}\,V_{i1},V_{2}\,V_{i2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma^{\prime}}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}
.

Hence, from now on, we assume
k\geq 1
. By the Anti-reduction lemma (Lemma
D.8
) (with
i_{1}=i_{2}=1
and
j=k
), it will suffice to show that
(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k-1}\llbracket d_{\sigma^{\prime}}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}
.

This will follow by our inductive hypothesis, which says that for any
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
, any natrual number
n
, and any
(\gamma_{1}^{\prime},\gamma_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{n}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x\sqsubseteq y:c\rrbracket
, we have

({M}[\gamma_{1}^{\prime}],{N}[\gamma_{2}^{\prime}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{n}\llbracket d_{\sigma^{\prime}}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

Let
\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\gamma_{1},V_{i1}/x
, let
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}=\gamma_{2},V_{i2}/y
. It is easily verified that
(\gamma_{1}^{\prime},\gamma_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{k-1}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x\sqsubseteq y:c\rrbracket
. (Doing so requires the monotonicity lemma, combined with the fact that
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
and that
k-1<k\leq i
).
Taking
n=k-1
above, and noting that
M^{\prime}={M}[\gamma_{1}^{\prime}]
and
N^{\prime}={N}[\gamma_{2}^{\prime}]
, it follows that
(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k-1}\llbracket d_{\sigma^{\prime}}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}
, as we wanted to show.

∎

Lemma D.25 (Congruence for Function Application).

Proof.

Suppose
\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}{M_{1}}\sqsubseteq{M_{2}}\in c\to_{d_{\sigma}}d
, and that
\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}{N_{1}}\sqsubseteq{N_{2}}\in c
.

We need to show that
\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}{M_{1}\,N_{1}}\sqsubseteq{M_{2}\,N_{2}}\in d
.

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show

\left({M_{1}\,N_{1}}[\gamma_{1}],{M_{2}\,N_{2}}[\gamma_{2}]\right)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

By Lemma
D.18
, it will suffice to show that

(1)
(M_{1}[\gamma_{1}],M_{2}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\to_{d_{\sigma}}d\rrbracket}
,
and that (2) for all
k\leq i
and

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\to_{d_{\sigma}}d\rrbracket
,
we have
(V_{1}\,N_{1},V_{1}\,N_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}
.

(1) follows immediately from our first top-level assumption.

To show (2), we again apply Lemma
D.18
. It follows from our second top-level assumption that
(N_{1}[\gamma_{1}],N_{2}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. Now let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and
(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c\rrbracket
. We claim that

(V_{1}\,V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}\,V_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

This holds since
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\to_{d_{\sigma}}d\rrbracket
and

(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.

∎

Lemma D.26 (Congruence for If).

Proof.

Suppose:

(1)

\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}\llbracket M\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket\in\texttt{bool}

(2)

\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}\llbracket N_{t}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket N_{t}^{\prime}\rrbracket\in c

(3)

\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}\llbracket N_{f}\rrbracket\sqsubseteq\llbracket N_{f}^{\prime}\rrbracket\in c

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show

\left({\texttt{if}\,\,M\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}}[\gamma_{1}],{\texttt{if}\,\,M^{\prime}\{N_{t}^{\prime}\}\{N_{f}^{\prime}\}}[\gamma_{2}]\right)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By Lemma
D.18
, it will suffice to show that
(1)
(\llbracket M\rrbracket[\gamma_{1}],\llbracket M^{\prime}\rrbracket[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{i}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}
, and
(2) for all
k\leq i
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket
, we have

(\texttt{if}\,\,V_{1}\{{N_{t}}[\gamma_{1}]\}\{{N_{f}}[\gamma_{1}]\}),(\texttt{if}\,\,V_{2}\{{N_{t}^{\prime}}[\gamma_{2}]\}\{{N_{f}^{\prime}}[\gamma_{2}]\})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

We note that (1) follows by our first top-level assumption. For (2), the assumption
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket
has two cases. If
V_{1}=V_{2}=\texttt{true}
, then by anti-reduction (Lemma
D.8
), it will suffice to show
({{N_{t}}[\gamma_{1}]},{{N_{t}^{\prime}}[\gamma_{2}]})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. But this follows from our second top-level assumption. Similarly, if
V_{1}=V_{2}=\texttt{false}
, then it suffices to show that
({{N_{f}}[\gamma_{1}]},{{N_{f}^{\prime}}[\gamma_{2}]})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
, which follows from our third top-level assumption.

∎

Lemma D.27 (Congruence for Let).

Proof.

This proof is similar to the function abstraction proof and is hence omitted.
∎

Lemma D.28 (Congruence for Raise).

Proof.

Let
c:A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{2}
and
d:B_{1}\sqsubseteq B_{2}
. Suppose
\varepsilon@c\leadsto d\in d_{\sigma}
and

\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}M_{1}\sqsubseteq M_{2}\in c.

We need to show that

\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}{\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(M_{1})}\sqsubseteq{\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(M_{2})}\in d.

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
and
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
. We will show

({\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(M_{1})}[\gamma_{1}],{\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(M_{2})}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

We apply Lemma
D.18
. We first claim that
({M_{1}}[\gamma_{1}],{M_{2}}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. This follows by assumption. Now, let
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\rrbracket
. We claim that

({\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{1})}[\gamma_{1}],{\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{2})}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

By Lemma
D.5
, it suffices to show that

({\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{1})}[\gamma_{1}],{\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{2})}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

We assert the second disjunct in the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket{}
, where we take
\varepsilon
to be
\varepsilon
(which we know by assumption is in
d_{\sigma}
), and we take
E^{l}=E^{r}=\bullet
and
V^{l}=V_{1}
,
V^{r}=V_{2}
.

We need to show that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket)_{k}
, and that

(x^{l}.(\bullet[x^{l}]),x^{r}.(\bullet[x^{r}]))\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket})

To this end, let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c\rrbracket
. We need to show

(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

But this follows by Lemma
D.6
.

∎

Lemma D.29 (Congruence for Handle).

\frac{M\sqsubseteq M^{\prime}:{d_{\sigma}}\,!\,{c}y:c\vdash N\sqsubseteq N^{\prime}:{d_{\tau}}\,!\,{d}\quad\quad\forall\varepsilon@d_{i}\leadsto d_{o}\in d_{\sigma}.(\varepsilon\notin\textrm{dom}(\phi)\wedge\varepsilon\notin\textrm{dom}(\phi^{\prime})\wedge\varepsilon:d_{i}\leadsto d_{o}\in d_{\tau})\vee\quad\quad x:d_{i},k:d_{o}\mathrel{\to_{d_{\tau}}}d\vdash\phi(\varepsilon)\sqsubseteq\phi^{\prime}(\varepsilon):{d_{\tau}}\,!\,{d}}{{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}\sqsubseteq{\texttt{handle}\,\,M^{\prime}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N^{\prime}\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}\}}:{d_{\tau}}\,!\,{d}}

Proof.

We use Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
). Assume that for
all
k\leq j
and all
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{G}^{\sim}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket)_{k}
and all

(M,M^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket)
, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}}[\gamma_{1}],

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,M^{\prime}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}.N^{\prime}\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}\}}}[\gamma_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{}_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket).

Let
(M,M^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Let
\sim\,\in\{<,>\}
and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
.
We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}}[\gamma_{1}],

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,M^{\prime}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}.N^{\prime}\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}\}}}[\gamma_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), it suffices to consider the following
cases:

•

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,V_{1}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}},

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,V_{2}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}.N^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\}}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction (Lemma
D.8
), it suffices to show that

(N[\gamma_{1}][V_{1}/x],N^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}][V_{2}/x^{\prime}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

This follows from the premise: if we let

\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\gamma_{1},V_{1}/x
and
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}=\gamma_{2},V_{2}/x^{\prime}
,
then it is easily checked that

(\gamma_{1}^{\prime},\gamma_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x\sqsubseteq x^{\prime}:c\rrbracket
.
Furthermore,
N[\gamma_{1}][V_{1}/x]=N[\gamma_{1}^{\prime}]
and likewise for

N[\gamma_{2}][V_{2}/x^{\prime}]
. The premise then implies that

(N[\gamma_{1}][V_{1}/x],N^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}][V_{2}/x^{\prime}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}
,
as needed.

•

Let
k\leq j
and let
\varepsilon@d_{i}\leadsto d_{o}\in d_{\sigma}

be an effect that is caught by either handler – i.e.,
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\phi)
or

\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\phi^{\prime})
. By the premise, it follows that
\varepsilon
is in both

\textrm{dom}(\phi)
and
\textrm{dom}(\phi^{\prime})
.

Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket)_{k}
. Let
E^{l}\#\varepsilon
and

E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be evaluation contexts such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{o}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}},

\displaystyle{{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}.N^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\}}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{1}][V^{l}/x][(\lambda y.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[y]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}})/k],

\displaystyle\phi^{\prime}(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{2}][V^{r}/x^{\prime}][(\lambda y.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[y]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}.N^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\}})/k^{\prime}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket).

To show this, we apply the premise, as follows.
Let
H_{1}={\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[y]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}
and

H_{2}={\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[y]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}.N^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}[\gamma_{2}]\}}
. Let

\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\gamma_{1},V^{l}/x_{i},(\lambda y.H_{1})/k_{i}
and let

\gamma_{2}^{\prime}=\gamma_{2},V^{r}/x_{i}^{\prime},(\lambda y.H_{2})/k_{i}^{\prime}
.
In order to apply the premise, we must prove that

(\gamma_{1}^{\prime},\gamma_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x_{i}\sqsubseteq x_{i}^{\prime}:d_{i},k_{i}\sqsubseteq k_{i}^{\prime}:d_{o}\to_{d_{\tau}}d\rrbracket
.

We first need to show that
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket)_{k}
.
This holds by assumption. We now need to show that

((\lambda y.H_{1}),(\lambda y.H_{2}))\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{o}\to_{d_{\tau}}d\rrbracket)_{k}.

To this end, let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V_{A},V_{B})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{o}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
. We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\lambda y.H_{1})\,V_{A},(\lambda y.H_{2})\,V_{B})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket)

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[V_{A}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}},{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[V_{B}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x^{\prime}.N^{\prime}\,\,|\,\,\phi^{\prime}\}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\tau}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket).

By the Löb induction hypothesis, it will suffice to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{E^{l}[V_{A}]},{E^{r}[V_{B}]})\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket).

Recall that by assumption, we have

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{o}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

Thus, it suffices to show that
(V_{A},V_{B})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{o}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
,
which is precisely our assumption.

∎

Note that we do not need to show soundness of the term precision congruence rules involving casts.
This will follow from the soundness of the upper and lower bound rules for casts.

Corollary D.30 (reflexivity).

Let
M
be a term such that
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\sigma}{M}:{A}
. We have

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}M\sqsubseteq M:A
.

Proof.

By induction on
M
, using the soundness of the term precision relation already proven.
∎

D.1.3.
Equational Rules

Lemma D.31 (Value substitution).

\frac{x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}:c\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}M\equiv N:dV\equiv V^{\prime}:c}{M[V/x_{1}]\equiv N[V^{\prime}/x_{2}]}

Proof.

Suppose for all
j
and all
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x^{l}\sqsubseteq x^{r}:c\rrbracket
, that

(x_{1}.M,x_{2}.N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}

and

(x_{2}.N,x_{1}.M)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

Further suppose that for all
j
,

(V,V^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket

and

(V^{\prime},V)\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket.

Let
j
be arbitrary, and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(M[V/x_{1}],N[V^{\prime}/x_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}

and

(N[V^{\prime}/x_{2}],M[V/x_{1}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket}.

The second statement is symmetric to the first, so we show only the first.

Let
\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=(\gamma_{1},x_{1}=V)
and let
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}=(\gamma_{2},x_{2}=V^{\prime})
.

Note that we have
M[\gamma_{1}^{\prime}]=M[\gamma_{1}][V/x_{1}]
and
N[\gamma_{2}^{\prime}]=N[\gamma_{2}][V^{\prime}/x_{2}]
,
by definition of substitution.

By our assumption, it is sufficient to show that
(\gamma_{1}^{\prime},\gamma_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq},x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}:c\rrbracket
.

For this, it sufficies to show that

(\gamma_{1}^{\prime}(x_{1}),\gamma_{2}^{\prime}(x_{2}))\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket
. But
\gamma_{1}^{\prime}(x_{1})=V
and

\gamma_{2}^{\prime}(x_{2})V^{\prime}
, so we are finished.

∎

Lemma D.32 (Monad Unit Left).

\frac{}{\texttt{let}\,\,x=y\,\texttt{in}\,\,N\equiv N[y/x]}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(\texttt{let}\,\,x=y\,\texttt{in}\,\,N,N[y/x])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Since
y
is a variable and hence a value, we have by the operational semantics that
