\texttt{let}\,\,x=y\,\texttt{in}\,\,N\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}N[y/x].

Thus, by anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

(N[y/x],N[y/x])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

But this follows by reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
).

∎

Lemma D.33 (Monad Unit Right).

\frac{}{\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,x\equiv M}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,x,M)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Since
x
is a variable and hence a value, we have by the operational semantics that

\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,x\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}M[x/x].

By definition of substitution,
M[x/x]=M
. Thus, by anti-reduction, it suffices
to show that

(M,M)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows by reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
).

∎

Lemma D.34 (Monad Associativity).

\frac{}{\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)\,\texttt{in}\,\,P\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,P}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)\,\texttt{in}\,\,P,\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,P)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

We apply Lemma
D.18
, taking

E_{1}=\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)\,\texttt{in}\,\,P
and

E_{2}=\texttt{let}\,\,x=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,P
.

We first need to show that
(M,M)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
,
which is true by reflexivity (Corolarry
D.30
).

Now, let
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We need to show that

(\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{1}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)\,\texttt{in}\,\,P,\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,P)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

According to the operational semantics, we have

(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{1}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}N[V_{1}/x].

Thus,

\texttt{let}\,\,y=(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{1}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)\,\texttt{in}\,\,P\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}\texttt{let}\,\,y=N[V_{1}/x]\,\texttt{in}\,\,P.

Similarly, we have

\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,P\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}(\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,P)[V_{2}/x]=\texttt{let}\,\,y=N[V_{2}/x]\,\texttt{in}\,\,P[V_{2}/x].

Note that since
x
does not occur in
P
, we have
P[V_{2}/x]=P
.

Now, by anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(\texttt{let}\,\,y=N[V_{1}/x]\,\texttt{in}\,\,P,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N[V_{2}/x]\,\texttt{in}\,\,P)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

We again apply Lemma
D.18
, this time with

E_{1}=\texttt{let}\,\,y=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,P
and

E_{2}=\texttt{let}\,\,y=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,P
.

We first need to show that

(N[V_{1}/x],N[V_{2}/x])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
This follows from reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
) and
value substitution (Lemma
D.31
) applied to our assumption
on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

Now let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and
(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{A}\llbracket k^{\prime}\rrbracket
. We need to show that

(\texttt{let}\,\,y=V_{1}^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,P,\texttt{let}\,\,y=V_{2}^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,P)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(P[V_{1}^{\prime}/y],P[V_{2}^{\prime}/y])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This again follows from reflexiviy and value substitution.

∎

Lemma D.35 (
\eta
-expansion for Booleans).

\frac{}{M[x:\texttt{bool}]\equiv\texttt{if}\,\,x\{M[\texttt{true}/x]\}\{M[{\texttt{false}/x}]\}}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma,x_{1}\sqsubseteq x_{2}:\texttt{bool}\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(M[\gamma_{1}],(\texttt{if}\,\,x\{M[\texttt{true}/x]\}\{M[{\texttt{false}/x}]\})[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By definition of substitution, this is equivalent to

(M[\gamma_{1}],(\texttt{if}\,\,\gamma\{_{\}}\{2\}(x){M[\texttt{true}/x][\gamma_{2}]}{M[{\texttt{false}/x}][\gamma_{2}]}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By our assumption on
\gamma_{1}
and
\gamma_{2}
, we have that either

\gamma_{1}(x_{1})=\gamma_{2}(x_{2})=\texttt{true}
or
\gamma_{1}(x_{1})=\gamma_{2}(x_{2})=\texttt{false}
.

We show only the former case; the latter is symmetric.
In the former case, we need to show

(M[\texttt{true}/x][\gamma_{1}],(\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{true}\{M[\texttt{true}/x][\gamma_{2}]\}\{M[{\texttt{false}/x}][\gamma_{2}]\}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it is sufficient to show

(M[\texttt{true}/x][\gamma_{1}],M[\texttt{true}/x][\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows by reflexivity.

∎

Lemma D.36 (Boolean
\beta
reduction - true).

\frac{}{\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{true}\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}\equiv N_{t}}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

((\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{true}\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\})[\gamma_{1}],N_{t}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(N_{t}[\gamma_{1}],N_{t}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This holds by reflexivity.

∎

Lemma D.37 (Boolean
\beta
reduction - false).

\frac{}{\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{false}\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}\equiv N_{f}}

Proof.

Precisely dual to the above proof.
∎

Lemma D.38 (Eval for If).

\frac{}{\texttt{if}\,\,M\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,x\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\}}\quad\textsc{Right=IfEval}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

((\texttt{if}\,\,M\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\})[\gamma_{1}],(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,x\{N_{t}\}\{N_{f}\})[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

We apply Lemma
D.18
, with

E_{1}=\texttt{if}\,\,\bullet\{N_{t}[\gamma_{1}]\}\{N_{f}[\gamma_{1}]\}
and

E_{2}=\texttt{let}\,\,x=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,\gamma_{2}(x)\{N_{t}[\gamma_{2}]\}\{N_{f}[\gamma_{2}]\}
.

We first need to show that
(M[\gamma_{1}],M[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}
.
This follows by reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
).

Now let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket
. We need to show that

((\texttt{if}\,\,V_{1}\{N_{t}[\gamma_{1}]\}\{N_{f}[\gamma_{1}]\}),(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,\gamma_{2}(x)\{N_{t}[\gamma_{2}]\}\{N_{f}[\gamma_{2}]\}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By definition of
\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket
, either
V_{1}=V_{2}=\texttt{true}
or
V_{1}=V_{2}=\texttt{false}
.
We consider the first case; the second is symmetric.

We need to show

((\texttt{if}\,\,\texttt{true}\{N_{t}[\gamma_{1}]\}\{N_{f}[\gamma_{1}]\}),(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\texttt{true}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{if}\,\,\gamma_{2}(x)\{N_{t}[\gamma_{2}]\}\{N_{f}[\gamma_{2}]\}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(N_{t}[\gamma_{1}],N_{t}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows by reflexivity.

∎

Lemma D.39 (
\beta
-reduction for functions).

\frac{}{(\lambda x.M)V\equiv M[V/x]}\quad\textsc{Right=FunBeta}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(((\lambda x.M)\,V)[\gamma_{1}],(M[V/x])[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Since
V
is a value, it suffices by anti-reduction to show that

(M[V/x][\gamma_{1}],M[V/x][\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows by reflexivity.

∎

Lemma D.40 (
\eta
-expansion for functions).

Let
V_{f}
be a value such that
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,\Delta\vdash_{\emptyset}{V}:{A\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B}
. We have

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}V_{f}\equiv(\lambda x.V_{f}x):(A\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B)
.

Proof.

Let
j
be arbitrary. We need to show

(V_{f},(\lambda x.V_{f}x))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\emptyset\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B\rrbracket}.

As these are values, it suffices by Lemma
D.6
to show that they
are related in
\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket A\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B\rrbracket
.
To this end, let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{i1},V_{i2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We claim that

(V_{f}\,V_{i1},(\lambda x.V_{f}x)\,V_{i2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it will suffice to show that

(V_{f}\,V_{i1},V_{f}\,V_{i2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
), we know that
(V_{f},V_{f})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\emptyset\rrbracket{A\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B}
,
and since
V_{f}
is a value, this means that
(V_{f},V_{f})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\to_{\sigma^{\prime}}B\rrbracket
.
This immediately implies the desired result, since
(V_{i1},V_{i2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

∎

Lemma D.41 (AppEval).

\frac{}{M\,N\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,x\,y}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

((M\,N)[\gamma_{1}],(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N\,\texttt{in}\,\,x\,y)[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\tau_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

We apply Lemma
D.18
, with
E_{1}=(\bullet\,N[\gamma_{2}])
and

E_{2}=\texttt{let}\,\,x=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N[\gamma_{2}]\,\texttt{in}\,\,x\,y
.

We first need to show that

(M[\gamma_{1}],M[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\tau_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket}
.
This follows by reflexivity.

Now let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket
.
We need to show that

((V_{1}\,N[\gamma_{1}]),(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,y=N[\gamma_{2}]\,\texttt{in}\,\,x\,y))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\tau_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

((V_{1}\,N[\gamma_{1}]),(\texttt{let}\,\,y=N[\gamma_{2}]\,\texttt{in}\,\,V_{2}\,y))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\tau_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

We again apply Lemma
D.18
, this time with

E_{1}=(V_{1}\,\bullet)
and
E_{2}=\texttt{let}\,\,y=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,V_{2}\,y
.

We need to show
(N[\gamma_{1}],N[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}
,
which holds by reflexivity. Now let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket
.
We need to show that

((V_{1}\,V_{1}^{\prime}),(\texttt{let}\,\,y=V_{2}^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,V_{2}\,y))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\tau_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

((V_{1}\,V_{1}^{\prime}),(V_{2}\,V_{2}^{\prime}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\tau_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

This follows from our assumptions on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
and on
V_{1}^{\prime}
and
V_{2}^{\prime}
.

∎

Lemma D.42 (HandleBetaRet).

\frac{}{{\texttt{handle}\,\,x\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}\equiv M[x/y]}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(({\texttt{handle}\,\,x\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M\,\,|\,\,\phi\}})[\gamma_{1}],(M[x/y])[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Since
x
is a value, the above handle term steps, and by anti-reduction it is sufficient to show

((M[x/y][\gamma_{1}]),(M[x/y])[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows by reflexivity.

∎

Lemma D.43 (HandleBetaRaise).

\frac{}{{\texttt{handle}\,\,({\texttt{let}\,\,o=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}})\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}\equiv\phi(\varepsilon)[\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}/k]}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,({\texttt{let}\,\,o=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}})\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M\,\,|\,\,\phi\}})[\gamma_{1}],

\displaystyle(\phi(\varepsilon)[\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}/k])[\gamma_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Let
E=\texttt{let}\,\,o=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{1}]
. Our goal is to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle(\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{2}][\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}/k]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Note that
E\#\varepsilon
. By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{1}][\lambda o^{\prime}.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E[o^{\prime}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}/k]),

\displaystyle(\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{2}][\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}/k]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

That is, we need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{1}][\lambda o^{\prime}.{\texttt{handle}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,o=o^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{1}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}/k]),

\displaystyle(\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{2}][\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}/k]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By ValSubst, it suffices to show (1)
for all related
(V_{f1},V_{f2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma}B\rrbracket
and

\gamma_{1}^{\prime}=\gamma_{1},V_{f1}/k
and
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}=\gamma_{2},V_{f2}/k
, we have

(\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{1}^{\prime}],\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{2}^{\prime}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket},

and (2),

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\lambda o^{\prime}.{\texttt{handle}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,o=o^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{1}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle(\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma}B\rrbracket}.

(1) follows from relfexivity. To show (2), we will use transitivity
(Lemma
D.66
).
If
\sim
is
<
, then note that by MonadUnitL we have

(\texttt{let}\,\,o=o^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{1}],N_{k}[\gamma_{2}][o^{\prime}/o])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket},

and by soundness of the congruence rules we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\lambda o^{\prime}.{\texttt{handle}\,\,\texttt{let}\,\,o=o^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{1}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle(\lambda o^{\prime}.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}][o^{\prime}/o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma}B\rrbracket}.

Then by transitivity, it will suffice to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\lambda o^{\prime}.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}][o^{\prime}/o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}),

\displaystyle(\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma}B\rrbracket}.

By congruence for lambdas, it suffices to show that, given related values

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket
, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}][o^{\prime}/o][V_{1}/o^{\prime}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}),

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}][V_{2}/o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma}B\rrbracket}.

This follows from the soundness of the congruence rules.

On the other hand, if
\sim
is
>
, then similarly by MonadUnitL we have

(\texttt{let}\,\,o=o^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{1}],N_{k}[\gamma_{1}][o^{\prime}/o])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

It then suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\lambda o^{\prime}.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{1}][o^{\prime}/o]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle(\lambda o.{\texttt{handle}\,\,N_{k}[\gamma_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.M[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma}B\rrbracket},

which again follows from the soundness of the congruence rules.

∎

Lemma D.44 (RaiseEval).

\frac{}{\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(M)\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

((\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(M))[\gamma_{1}],\,(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

We apply Monadic Bind (Lemma
D.18
), with

E_{1}=\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\bullet)
and

E_{2}=\texttt{let}\,\,x=\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x)
.

We first need to show that
(M[\gamma_{1}],M[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\tau\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
This follows from reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
).

Now let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We need to show that

((\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{1}))[\gamma_{1}],\,(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

As
V_{2}
is a value, the above let term steps. By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

((\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{1})),\,(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{2})))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows from our assumption on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
and
the soundness of the term congruence rule for raise (Lemma
D.28
).

∎

Lemma D.45 (HandleEmpty).

\frac{}{{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\emptyset\}}\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,N}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.

Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N\,\,|\,\,\emptyset\}})[\gamma_{1}],

\displaystyle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,N)[\gamma_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By Monadic Bind (Lemma
D.18
) and the fact that
neither evaluation context catches any effects, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\texttt{handle}\,\,V_{1}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,x.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\emptyset\}},

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,N[\gamma_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket},

where
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket B\rrbracket
.
By anti-reduction, it will suffice to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{N[\gamma_{1}][V_{1}/x]},N[\gamma_{2}][V_{2}/x])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Using ValSubst, the result follows by reflexivity and our assumption on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

∎

Lemma D.46.

\frac{\forall\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\phi).~\psi(\varepsilon)=\phi(\varepsilon)\forall\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\psi).\varepsilon\not\in\textrm{dom}(\phi)\Rightarrow\psi(\varepsilon)=k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))}{{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\phi\}}\equiv{\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N\,\,|\,\,\psi\}}:{\sigma}\,!\,{B}}\quad\textsc{Right=HandleExt}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.

The proof is by Löb induction. We assume that

(({\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,_{.}1\,\,|\,\,^{\prime}\}}yN\phi)[\gamma_{1}],({\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,_{.}2\,\,|\,\,^{\prime}\}}yN\psi)[\gamma_{2}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{j}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

for all
k\leq j
,
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{G}^{\sim}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket)_{k}
and

(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)
.

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(({\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,_{.}1\,\,|\,\,y\}}N\phi)[\gamma_{1}],({\texttt{handle}\,\,M\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,_{.}2\,\,|\,\,y\}}N\psi)[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}

for all
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.

We apply Monadic Bind (Lemma
D.18
).
It suffices to consider the following cases:

•

Let
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We need to show that

(({\texttt{handle}\,\,V_{1}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),({\texttt{handle}\,\,V_{2}\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows by anti-reduction and reflexivity.

•

Let
k\leq j
and let
\varepsilon\in\sigma
be an effect caught by either handler,
i.e.,
\varepsilon
is in
\textrm{dom}(\phi)
or
\textrm{dom}(\psi)
.
Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
, and let
E^{l}\#\varepsilon

and
E^{r}\#\varepsilon
such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket})
.

We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

If
\varepsilon\in\textrm{dom}(\phi)
, then by the premise, we have

\psi(\varepsilon)=\phi(\varepsilon)
, so both sides step, and it
suffices by anti-reduction to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{1}][V^{l}/x][(\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}})/k],

\displaystyle\phi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{2}][V^{r}/x][(\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}})/k])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

By ValSubst, it suffices to show that
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
,
which is true by assumption, and that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle(\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\to_{\sigma}B\rrbracket)_{k}.

By congruence for lambdas, it suffices to show that, given values

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[V_{1}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}},

\displaystyle{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[V_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

This follows by the Löb induction hypothesis and our assumption on

E^{l}
and
E^{r}
.

Now assume that
\varepsilon\notin\textrm{dom}(\phi)
. Then note that the first handle term
does not step, while the second handle term steps to

\psi(\varepsilon)[\gamma_{2}][V^{r}/x][(\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}})/k].

By the premise, we have
\psi(\varepsilon)=k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))
.
Thus, by anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle(k(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(x))[\gamma_{2})[V^{r}/x][(\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}})/k])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

That is, it will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle((\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}})\,(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V)^{r})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Neither term steps, so it suffices to show they are related
in
\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}
.

We need to show that
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
,
which is true by assumption, and that given
k^{\prime}\leq k
and related values

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[V_{1}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle((\lambda z.{\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[z]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}})\,V_{2}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{l}[V_{1}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{1}]\,\,|\,\,\phi[\gamma_{1}]\}}),

\displaystyle({\texttt{handle}\,\,E^{r}[V_{2}]\,\{\texttt{ret}\,\,y.N[\gamma_{2}]\,\,|\,\,\psi[\gamma_{2}]\}}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

This follows by the Löb induction hypothesis and our assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
.

∎

D.1.4.
Cast, Error, and Subtyping Properties

Lemma D.47 (Err-bot).

\frac{M:{{d_{\sigma}}^{r}}\,!\,{c^{r}}}{\mho\sqsubseteq M:{d_{\sigma}}\,!\,{c}}

Proof.

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show

(\mho[\gamma_{1}],M[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

This follows from the definition of the logical relation:
If
\sim
is
<
(counting steps on the left), then we are finished by
the definition of the
\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket\rrbracket{}
relation, because
\mho\mathrel{\mapsto^{0}}\mho
.

If
\sim
is
>
(counting steps on the right), then we are similarly
finished, because
M\mathrel{\mapsto^{0}}M
and the left-hand term is
\mho
.

∎

Lemma D.48 (Err-strict).

E[\mho]\equiv\mho

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
,
d_{\sigma}
, and
c
be arbitrary. We need to show

(E[\mho],\mho)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it is sufficient to show

(\mho,\mho)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket},

which is easily seen to hold by definition of the logical relation.

∎

Lemma D.49 (Monotonicity of Subtyping).

If
c\leq d
then
\mathcal{V}\llbracket c\rrbracket\subseteq\mathcal{V}\llbracket d\rrbracket

Further, if
R\subseteq S
then
\mathcal{K}\llbracket d\rrbracket R\subseteq\mathcal{K}\llbracket c\rrbracket S
,

Further, if
c_{\sigma}\leq d_{\sigma}
then both

•

\mathcal{E}\llbracket c\rrbracket R\subseteq\mathcal{E}\llbracket d\rrbracket S

•

\mathcal{R}\llbracket c\rrbracket R\subseteq\mathcal{R}\llbracket d\rrbracket S

Proof.

By mutual induction on the subtyping proofs.
First the type subtyping cases:

(1)

\texttt{bool}\leq\texttt{bool}
: trivial.

(2)

c_{i}\to_{c_{e}}c_{o}\leq d_{i}to_{d_{e}}d_{o}
. Assume

(V_{f},V_{f}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket c_{i}\to_{c_{e}}c_{o}\rrbracket
, we need to show

(V_{f},V_{f}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket d_{i}\to_{d_{e}}d_{o}\rrbracket
. Let
(V_{i},V_{i}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket d_{i}\rrbracket
. Then by inductive hypothesis,
(V_{i},V_{i}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{V}\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket
. Therefore
(V_{f}V_{i},V_{f}^{\prime}V_{i}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}\llbracket c_{e}\rrbracket\mathcal{V}\llbracket c_{o}\rrbracket
and the result follows by the two inductive
hypotheses.

The
\mathcal{K}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
case follows by a similar argument to the
function case.

The
\mathcal{E}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
case follows by inductive hypothesis.

Next the
\mathcal{R}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
cases:

(1)

{?}\leq{?}
: trivial

(2)

\frac{c\leq\Sigma}{c\leq{?}}
: trivial by definition of
\mathcal{R}\llbracket{?}\rrbracket

(3)

\frac{c\leq d}{c\leq Inj(d)}
: trivial by definition of
\mathcal{R}\llbracket Inj(i,d)\rrbracket

(4)

\frac{c\leq d}{Inj(c)\leq Inj(d)}
: trivial by definition of
\mathcal{R}\llbracket Inj(i,d)\rrbracket

(5)

\frac{\textrm{dom}(d_{c})\subseteq\textrm{dom}(d^{\prime}_{c})\hskip 8.19447pt\hskip 8.19447pt\forall\varepsilon:c\leadsto d\in d_{c}.\varepsilon:c^{\prime}\leadsto d^{\prime}\in d^{\prime}_{c}\wedge c\leq c^{\prime}\wedge d^{\prime}\leq d}{d_{c}\leq d^{\prime}_{c}}
:
Follows using Löb induction by the monotonicity of subtyping for the
\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
and

\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket
relations.

∎

We next prove generalized versions of the cast properties ValUpL, ValUpR, ValDnL, ValDnR, EffUpL, EffUpR, EffDnL, EffDnR.
These are proved simultaneously by induction on the type precision derivation and by Löb-induction.

Lemma D.50 (ValUpR-general).

\frac{c:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\quad\quad e:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}{M}\sqsubseteq{N}:c}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\sigma}}{M}\sqsubseteq{\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle N}:c\circ e}

Proof.

We need to show that

({M},{\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle N})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\circ e\rrbracket}.

The proof is by induction on the precision derivation
e
.
By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), with
E_{1}=\bullet
and

E_{2}=\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet
, it suffices to show

({V_{1}},{\langle{A^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle V_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\circ e\rrbracket},

where
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.
We continue by cases on
e
.

•

Case
e=\texttt{bool}
. We have
A=A^{\prime}=A^{\prime\prime}=\texttt{bool}
, and
c=\texttt{bool}
. Thus
c\circ e=\texttt{bool}
.

Examining the operational semantics, we see that

({\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle})({V_{1}})\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}{V_{1}}.

Thus, by anti-reduction, it suffices to show

({V_{1}},{V_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}.

This is true by assumption and Lemma
D.6
.

•

Case
e=e_{i}\to_{e_{\sigma}}e_{o}
. We have

A^{\prime}=A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime}
and

A^{\prime\prime}=A_{i}^{\prime\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime\prime}
, and also

e_{i}:A_{i}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A_{i}^{\prime\prime}
and

e_{o}:A_{o}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A_{o}^{\prime\prime}
.

By inversion, we see that
c=c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}
.
Thus, we have that

c\circ e=(c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o})\circ(e_{i}\to_{e_{\sigma}}e_{o})=(c_{i}\circ e_{i})\to_{c_{\sigma}\circ e_{\sigma}}(c_{o}\circ e_{o})
.

We need to show that

({V_{1}},{\langle{(A_{i}^{\prime\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime})}\rangle V_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket(c_{i}\circ e_{i})\to_{c_{\sigma}\circ e_{\sigma}}(c_{o}\circ e_{o})\rrbracket}.

As both terms are values, it suffices by Lemma
D.6

to show they are related in
{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket(c_{i}\circ e_{i})\to_{c_{\sigma}\circ e_{\sigma}}(c_{o}\circ e_{o})\rrbracket}
.
To this end, let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and
(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c_{i}\circ e_{i}\rrbracket
.
We need to show that

({V_{1}}\,V^{l},(\langle{(A_{i}^{\prime\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime})}\rangle V_{2})\,V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c_{\sigma}\circ e_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{o}\circ e_{o}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

({V_{1}}\,V^{l},\langle{A_{o}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{A}^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle V^{r}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c_{\sigma}\circ e_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{o}\circ e_{o}\rrbracket}.

By the induction hypothesis applied twice, it suffices to show

({V_{1}}\,V^{l},(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle V^{r}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{o}\rrbracket}.

Finally, it suffices by the soundness of the term precision congruence rule for
function application (Lemma
D.25
to show
that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}\rrbracket
, and that

(V^{l},\langle{A_{i}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{i}^{\prime\prime}}\rangle V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

The former is true by our assumption on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.
The latter follows by the induction hypothesis and our assumption on
V^{l}
and
V^{r}
.

∎

Lemma D.51 (ValUpL-general).

\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}c:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}e:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}{M}\sqsubseteq{N}:c\circ e}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}{\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M}\sqsubseteq{N}:e}

Proof.

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show that

({\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M}[\gamma_{1}],{N}[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket e\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), with

E_{1}=\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\bullet
and

E_{2}=\bullet
, it suffices to show

({\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{1}},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket e\rrbracket},

where
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\circ e\rrbracket
.

We continue by cases on
c
.
The case
c=\texttt{bool}
is similar to that in the previous lemma, so we skip to considering the case

c=c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}
. By inversion, we see that
e=e_{i}\to_{e_{\sigma}}e_{o}
.

We have

A=A_{i}\to_{\hat{\sigma}}A_{o}
and

A^{\prime}=A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime}
, and also
Thus, we have that
c\circ e=(c_{i}\circ e_{i})\to_{c_{\sigma}\circ e_{\sigma}}(c_{o}\circ e_{o})
.

We need to show that

({\langle{(A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\hat{\sigma}}A_{o})}\rangle M}[\gamma_{1}],N[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket e_{i}\to_{e_{\sigma}}e_{o}\rrbracket}.

Similar to before, it suffices to show that these terms are related at
\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket e_{i}\to_{e_{\sigma}}e_{o}\rrbracket
.
This is similar to proof of the previous lemma, and hence omitted.

∎

Lemma D.52 (ValDnL-general).

\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}c:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}e:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}M\sqsubseteq N:e}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq N:c\circ e}\par

Proof.

This proof is dual to the proof of ValUpR-general (Lemma
D.50
) and is hence omitted.
∎

Lemma D.53 (ValDnR-general).

\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}c:A\sqsubseteq A^{\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}e:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq A^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}M\sqsubseteq N:c\circ e}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}M\sqsubseteq\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A^{\prime\prime}}\rangle N:c}\par

Proof.

This proof is dual to the proof of ValUpL-general (Lemma
D.51
) and is hence omitted.
∎

Lemma D.54 (EffUpR-general).

\frac{d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}\quad\quad d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}{M}\sqsubseteq{N}:c}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}}{M}\sqsubseteq{\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N}:c}

Proof.

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
. We need to show that

({M},{{\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

We prove this statement by Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
).
That is, assume for all
k\leq j
and all

(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
,
we have

(M^{\prime},\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

Let
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. We need to show

(M,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

We proceed by cases on
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
. The case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}={?}
is immediate,
so consider
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}=\texttt{inj}(d_{c})
,
where
d_{c}:\sigma_{c}\sqsubseteq\Sigma\mid_{\textrm{supp}(\sigma_{c})}
.
In this case, we know that
\sigma^{\prime\prime}={?}
. Furthermore, we have

d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}=d_{\sigma}\circ(\texttt{inj}(d_{c}))=\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c}).

Thus, we need to show

({M},{{\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c})\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), it will suffice to consider the following cases:

•

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\rrbracket
. We need to show

(V_{1},{\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle V_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c})\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c})\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

As
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are values, it suffices by Lemma
D.6
to
show that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\rrbracket
, which is true by assumption.

•

Let
k\leq j
and
\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in d_{\sigma}

be an effect that is caught by
{\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet}
.
Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
, and
let
E^{l}\#\varepsilon
and
E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be evaluation contexts such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
. We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c})\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{d_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\rangle V^{r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[y])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c})\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime r}
be the term to which
\langle{c_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\rangle V^{r}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{d_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[y])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c})\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

As neither term steps, it suffices to show they are related in

\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\texttt{inj}(d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c})\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.
.
To this end, we need to show (1)
(V^{l},V^{\prime r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\circ c^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
,
and (2) given
k^{\prime}\leq k
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\circ d^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
, we
have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[V_{1}],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{d_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\rangle V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[y])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{Inj}({I},{d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c}})\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

To show (1), it suffices by forward reduction to show that

(V^{l},\langle{c_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\rangle V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\circ c^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
.
This follows inductively from ValUpR (which we are proving simultaneously and can
therefore apply at smaller types), and our assumption on
V^{l}
and
V^{r}
.

To show (2), let
V_{2}^{\prime}
be the value to which

\langle{d_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\rangle V_{2}
steps.
It suffices by anti-reduction to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[V_{1}],{\langle{{?}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime}]})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{Inj}({I},{d_{\sigma}\circ d_{c}})\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

By the Löb induction hypothesis, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[V_{1}],{E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime}]})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

By our assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},V_{2}^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
.
By forward reduction, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},\langle{d_{\varepsilon}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{\varepsilon}^{?}}\rangle V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket).

Now inductively by ValDnR, it suffices to show

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\circ d^{\prime}_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
,
which is our assumption.

The case where
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
is a concrete effect precision derivation is similar to the above.

∎

Lemma D.55 (EffUpL-general).

\frac{d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}\quad\quad d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}}{M}\sqsubseteq{N}:c}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}^{\prime}}\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\sqsubseteq N:c}

Proof.

This is proved similarly to the above.
∎

Lemma D.56 (EffDnL-general).

\frac{d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}\quad\quad d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}^{\prime}}M\sqsubseteq N:c}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}}}\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq N:c}

Proof.

We prove this by Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
).
That is, assume for all
k\leq j
and all

(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
,
we have

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
, and let

(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. We need to show

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
) and the fact that effect casts are the
identity on values, it will suffice to show the following:

Let
k\leq j
and
\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in d_{\sigma}^{\prime}

be an effect that is caught by
{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet}
.
Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
, and
let
E^{l}\#\varepsilon
and
E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be evaluation contexts such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
. We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})]N)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

Note that if
\varepsilon\notin\sigma
, then the left hand side steps to
\mho
,
in which case we are finished by ErrBot (Lemma
D.47
).
Otherwise, the proof proceeds alalogously to EffUpR (Lemma
D.54
),
with upcasts and downcasts interchanged.

∎

Lemma D.57 (EffDnR-general).

\frac{d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}\quad\quad d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}\quad\quad\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma^{\prime}}}M\sqsubseteq N:c}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{d_{\sigma}}M\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle N:c}

Proof.

We prove this statement by Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
).
That is, assume for all
k\leq j
and all

(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
,
we have

(M^{\prime},\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\rrbracket
, and let

(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
. We need to show

(M,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
) and the fact that effect casts are the
identity on values, it will suffice to show the following:

Let
k\leq j
and
\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}

be an effect that is caught by
{\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\bullet}
.
Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
, and
let
E^{l}\#\varepsilon
and
E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be evaluation contexts such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
. We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

First note that by Lemma
D.21
, there exist

c_{1}
,
c_{2}
,
d_{1}
, and
d_{2}
such that

c_{\varepsilon}=c_{1}\circ c_{2}
and
d_{\varepsilon}=d_{1}\circ d_{2}
and

\varepsilon@c_{1}\leadsto d_{1}\in d_{\sigma}
and
\varepsilon@c_{2}\leadsto d_{2}\in d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
.
In particular, this that
\varepsilon\in\sigma^{\prime}
, so the downcast from
\sigma^{\prime\prime}
to
\sigma^{\prime}
does not fail.
Let
c^{L}=c_{1}^{l}(=c_{\varepsilon}^{l})
,

c^{M}=c_{1}^{r}=c_{2}^{l}
,
and
c^{R}=c_{2}^{r}(=c_{\varepsilon}^{r})
, and likewise define
d^{L},d^{M}
and
d^{R}
.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{d^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{M}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{R}}\rangle V^{r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[y])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime r}
be the term to which
\langle{c^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{R}}\rangle V^{r}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{d^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{M}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[y])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

As neither term steps, it suffices to show they are related in

\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.
To this end, we need to show (1)
(V^{l},V^{\prime r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{1}\rrbracket)_{k}
,
and (2) given
k^{\prime}\leq k
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{1}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
, we
have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[V_{1}],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{d^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{M}}\rangle V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[y])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

(1) follows from forward reduction and the inductive hypothesis for value types.
To show (2), let
V_{2}^{\prime}
be the value to which

\langle{d^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{M}}\rangle V_{2}
steps.
It suffices by anti-reduction to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[V_{1}],{\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime}]})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

By the Löb induction hypothesis, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[V_{1}],{E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime}]})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

By our assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},V_{2}^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
.
By forward reduction, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},\langle{d^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{M}}\rangle V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket).

Now inductively by ValUpR, it suffices to show

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{1}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
,
which is our assumption.

The case where
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
is a concrete effect precision derivation is similar to the above.
∎

Lemma D.58 (ValUpEval).

\frac{}{\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x}

Proof.

We show one direction of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.
Let
j
be arbitrary and let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\Gamma\rrbracket
.
We need to show

((\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M)[\gamma_{1}],\,(\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x)[\gamma_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By Monadic Bind (Lemma
D.18
) and reflexivity, it will suffice to
show that for all
k\leq j
let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
, we have

((\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{1}),\,(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle x))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

((\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{1}),\,(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{2}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By congruence, it suffices to show

(V_{1},\,V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

This follows from our assumption on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

∎

Lemma D.59 (ValDnEval).

\frac{}{\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle M\equiv\texttt{let}\,\,x=M\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle x}

Proof.

Dual to the above. ∎

Lemma D.60 (cast-retraction).

let
A\sqsubseteq B
and
\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
, and let
c:A\sqsubseteq B

and
d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
.
Let
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vdash_{\sigma}M\sqsubseteq N:A
.
The following hold:

(1)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M\sqsubseteq N:A

(2)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\sqsubseteq N:A

Proof.

We prove stronger, “pointwise" version of the above statemenets. Namely, we assume

(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
,
and show, for example, that

(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.

The proof is by simultaneous induction on the derivations
c
and
d_{\sigma}
.

(1)

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.
Suppose
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
We need to show

(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), it suffices to show that

(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket},

where
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

We proceed by induction on the precision derivation
c
. If
c=\texttt{bool}
, then we need to show

(\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}.

According to the operational semantics, we have that

\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle V_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{2}}V_{1}.

So by anti-reduction (Lemma
D.8
), it suffices to show that

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}
, which follows from our assumption.

If
c=c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}
, then
A=A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}
and
B=B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o}
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(\langle{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket}.

As both of these are values, it suffices to show that they are related in

\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket
. To this end, let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let

(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket
. We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\langle{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\rangle\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{1})\,V^{l},

\displaystyle V_{2}\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

The former term steps, so by anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{A_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle((\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{1})\,\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle V_{2}\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime l}
be the value to which
\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{l}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{A_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime l})),

\displaystyle V_{2}\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

We will appeal to transitivity (Lemma
D.66
).
We continue by cases on
\sim
. First assume
\sim
is
<
.
Let
V^{\prime r}
be the value to which
\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{r}
steps. If we show (1)

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{A_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime l})),

\displaystyle\langle{A_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

and (2)

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{A_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})),

\displaystyle V_{2}\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket},

then we will be finished by transitivity.

To show (1), first note that by monotonicity of casts (Lemma
D.65
),
it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime l})),

\displaystyle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{o}\rrbracket}.

Then by commutativity of casts (Corollary
D.63
),
it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime l}),

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{B}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By monotonicity of casts again, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime l}),

\displaystyle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By soundness of the precision rule for function application, it suffices to
show that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})\rrbracket
and that

(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime l},\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}
.
The former holds by assumption, and to show the latter, it suffices by forward reduction to show

(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{l},\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}
. This follows from the inductive hypothesis and
assumption on
V^{l}
and
V^{r}
.

To show (2), it suffices by the inductive hypothesis applied twice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})),V_{2}\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket},

By forward reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})),V_{2}\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket},

By soundness of function application, it suffices to show
that
V_{2}
is related to itself at
\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})\rrbracket

and that
(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r},V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}
.
The former holds by reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
), and to show the
latter it suffices by forward reduction to show that

(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{r},V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket},

which follows by the inductive hypothesis and reflexivity.

The case when
\sim
is
<
is analogous.

(2)

Let
(\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2})\in\mathcal{G}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.
We use Löb induction. We assume that for all
k\leq j
and all related terms

(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)
, we have

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

Let
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
. We need to show that

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), it suffices to consdier
the following cases:

(a)

Let
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We need to show

\displaystyle(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}

This follows by anti-reduction and assumption.

(b)

Let
k\leq j
and let

\varepsilon@C\leadsto D\in\sigma
.
Let
C^{\prime}
and
D^{\prime}
be the types such that

\varepsilon@C^{\prime}\leadsto D^{\prime}\in\sigma^{\prime}
.
Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket)_{k}

and let
E^{l}\#\varepsilon
and
E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}_{)}\llbracket D\rrbracket_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])
