\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

The first term steps, so by anti-reduction it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{\prime}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C}\rangle V^{l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x]),

\displaystyle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime l}
be the value to which
\langle{C^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C}\rangle V^{l}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{D^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C^{\prime}}\rangle V^{\prime l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{\prime}}\rangle y\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Let
y^{\prime}
be the value to which
\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{\prime}}\rangle y
steps.
Let
V^{\prime\prime l}
be the value to which
\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C^{\prime}}\rangle V^{\prime l}
steps.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{D^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime\prime l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[y^{\prime}]},

\displaystyle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Neither term steps, so it suffices to show they are related in

\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
. To this end, we first
show that
(V^{\prime\prime l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket)_{k}
.
By forward reduction, it suffices to show that

(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{C^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C}\rangle V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket)_{k}
.
This follows from the inductive hypothesis for value types and our assumption on

V^{l}
and
V^{r}
.

We now show that, given
k^{\prime}\leq k
and values

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket D\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{D^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle V_{1}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[y^{\prime}]},

\displaystyle E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

Let
V_{1}^{\prime}
be the value to which
\langle{D^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle V_{1}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=V_{1}^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[y^{\prime}]},

\displaystyle E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

By forward reduction, it will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=V_{1}^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{\prime}}\rangle y]},

\displaystyle E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

By anti-reduction, it will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{\prime}}\rangle V_{1}^{\prime}]}},E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

By the Löb induction hypothesis, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{\prime}}\rangle V_{1}^{\prime}],E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

By forward reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E^{l}[\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{D^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle V_{1}],E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

By the induction hypothesis for value types, it suffices to show

(E^{l}[V_{1}],E^{r}[V_{2}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

This follows by our assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
.

∎

Lemma D.61 (Gradual subtyping).

Let
c:A\sqsubseteq B
and
c^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
where
A\leq A^{\prime}
and
B\leq B^{\prime}
.
Let
d_{\sigma}:\sigma_{1}\sqsubseteq\sigma_{2}
and
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
where

\sigma_{1}\leq\sigma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\sigma_{2}\leq\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
.
Suppose
M\equiv N
.
The following hold:

(1)

\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\tau}}M\sqsubseteq N:A}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\tau}}\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle N:B^{\prime}}

(2)

\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\tau}}M\sqsubseteq N:B}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{d_{\tau}}\langle{A^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B^{\prime}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle N:A^{\prime}}

(3)

\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{1}}M\sqsubseteq N:d}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle N:d}

(4)

\frac{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{2}}M\sqsubseteq N:d}{\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\langle{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle N:d}

Proof.

By simultaneous induction on the derivation
c^{\prime}:A^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B^{\prime}
and
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma_{2}^{\prime}
.

(1)

We need to show

(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), with

E_{1}=\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\bullet
and
E_{2}=\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet
, it suffices to show the following.

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{1},\langle{B^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A^{\prime}}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B^{\prime}\rrbracket}.

We continue by cases on
c^{\prime}
.

Case
c^{\prime}=\texttt{bool}
. Then by inversion on the rules for subtyping of precision derivations,
we have
c=\texttt{bool}
.

We need to show

(\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle V_{1},\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}

This follows by anti-reduction and our assumption on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

Case
c^{\prime}=c_{i}^{\prime}\to_{c_{\sigma}^{\prime}}c_{o}^{\prime}:A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime}\sqsubseteq B_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{B}^{\prime}}B_{o}^{\prime}
.

By inversion on the rules for subtyping for precision derivations, we have that

c=c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}
, where

c_{i}^{\prime}\leq c_{i}
, and
c_{\sigma}\leq c_{\sigma}^{\prime}
, and
c_{o}\leq c_{o}^{\prime}
.

Our assumption then becomes
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(\langle{B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}}\rangle V_{1},\langle{B_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{B}^{\prime}}B_{o}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime}}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{B}^{\prime}}B_{o}^{\prime}\rrbracket}.

Since both terms are values, it suffices to show they are related in

\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket B_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{B}^{\prime}}B_{o}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
Let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket B_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\langle{B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}}\rangle V_{1})\,V^{l},

\displaystyle(\langle{B_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{B}^{\prime}}B_{o}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime}}\rangle V_{2})\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{o}^{\prime}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle\langle{B_{o}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}^{\prime}}\rangle V^{r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{B}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{o}^{\prime}\rrbracket}.

By the induction hypothesis applied twice, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}^{\prime}}\rangle V^{r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}^{\prime}\rrbracket}.

By soundness of the term precision congruence rule for function application (Lemma
D.25
),
it suffices to show that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket A_{i}^{\prime}\to_{\sigma_{A}^{\prime}}A_{o}^{\prime}\rrbracket
,
and that

(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l},\langle{A_{i}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}^{\prime}}\rangle V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket}.

The former holds by assumption.
To show the latter, it suffices by the admissible direction of gradual subtyping rule ValDnSub (item (2)
in Lemma
B.1
), whose proof does not depend on the present lemma,
to show that
(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket B_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket
. This is true by assumption.

(2)

Similar to the above.

(3)

We need to show

(\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle M,\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

We use Löb induction. That is, we assume as our induction hypothesis that

(\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle M^{\prime},\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle N^{\prime})\in{\blacktriangleright}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket)_{j}{(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket)},

for all
(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{j}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket)
,
and we show that under this assumption, we have

(\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle M,\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}

for all
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Using Monadic Bind (Lemma
D.18
), we have the following cases:

•

Let
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle V_{1},\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

This follows by anti-reduction and our assumption on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

•

Let
\varepsilon@c_{i}\leadsto d_{i}\in\sigma_{1}
be an effect caught by

\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet
.
Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{i}^{l}\rrbracket)_{k}
, and let

(E^{l},E^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{i}^{l}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

We continue by cases on subtyping of effect precision derivations.
We show only the case
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}
is a concrete effect precision set
d_{c}^{\prime}
;
the other cases follow immediately or reduce to this one.

By inversion, we have
d_{\sigma}
is also a concrete effect precision set
d_{c}
where

\textrm{dom}(d_{c})\subseteq\textrm{dom}(d^{\prime}_{c})
and
for all
\varepsilon:c\leadsto d\in d_{c}
,

\varepsilon:c^{\prime}\leadsto d^{\prime}\in d^{\prime}_{c}
and
c\leq c^{\prime}
and
d^{\prime}\leq d
.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{d_{i}^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{i}^{r}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c_{i}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{i}^{l}}\rangle V^{l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{d_{i}^{\prime l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{i}^{\prime r}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c_{i}^{\prime r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{i}^{\prime l}}\rangle V^{r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[x])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{)}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}),

By congruence for Let, it suffices to show (1)

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\langle{d_{i}^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{i}^{r}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c_{i}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{i}^{l}}\rangle V^{l})},

\displaystyle{\langle{d_{i}^{\prime l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{d_{i}^{\prime r}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c_{i}^{\prime r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{i}^{\prime l}}\rangle V^{r})})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{)}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}),

and (2) for
(V_{1},V_{2})\in{\blacktriangleright}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{i}\rrbracket)_{k}
we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle E^{l}[V_{1}]},

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[V_{2}]})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{)}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}),

To show (1), first note that by the induction hypothesis for value types,

(\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c_{i}^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{i}^{l}}\rangle V^{l}),\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c_{i}^{\prime r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{c_{i}^{\prime l}}\rangle V^{r}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{i}^{\prime}\rrbracket},

and by the induction hypothesis for value types again, (1) follows.
To show (2), note that
E^{l}[x^{l}]
and
E^{r}[x^{r}]
are related by
assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
. So we may apply the Löb induction hypothesis
to reach the desired conclusion.

(4)

We again use Löb induction and monadic bind. In the related raises case of the bind lemma,
we let

\varepsilon@c_{i}\leadsto d_{i}\in\sigma_{2}
be an effect caught by

\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet
. We let

(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c_{i}^{l}\rrbracket)_{k}
, and let

(E^{l},E^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d_{i}^{l}\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
.

We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma_{2}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],\langle{\sigma_{2}^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{1}^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{2}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

If
\varepsilon\notin\sigma_{1}
, then both sides step to
\mho
. Since
\mho
is
related to itself by ErrBot (Lemma
D.47
), we are finished by anti-reduction.

Otherwise, the proof proceeds analogously to that of the previous case, with
upcasts and downcasts interchanged.

∎

Lemma D.62 (effect casts commute with pure function values).

Let
E
be an evaluation context such that
(1) for all
\sigma
,
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,{\Gamma}\,\,|\,\,{\bullet:({\sigma}\,!\,{A})}\vdash_{\sigma}{E}:{B}
,
and such that (2)
E\#\varepsilon
for all
\varepsilon\in\Sigma
.
Furthermore, suppose that (3) for all values
V
, there exists a value
V^{\prime}
such that
E[V]\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}V^{\prime}
.

Let
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{2}}M\equiv N:A
.

Then
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma_{1}}E[\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle M]\equiv\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E[N]:B
,
and likewise for upcasts.

Proof.

We show the statement for downcasts only; the proof for upcasts is similar.
Additionally, we show only one of the directions of the equivalence; the other is symmetric.

We need to show

(E[\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle M],\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E[N])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

We apply monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
) with
E_{1}=E[\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle\bullet]
and

E_{2}=\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E
. By assumption on
M
and
N
, will suffice to consider the following
cases.

•

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.
We need to show

(E[\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle V_{1}],\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E[V_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By the operational semantics, we have
E[\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle V_{1}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}E[V_{1}]
.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(E[V_{1}],\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E[V_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Furthermore, there exist
i_{1}
and
i_{2}
and values
V_{1}^{\prime}
and
V_{2}^{\prime}
such that

E[V_{1}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{1}}}V_{1}^{\prime}
and

E[V_{2}]\mathrel{\mapsto^{i_{2}}}V_{2}^{\prime}
.

We also have
\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle V_{2}^{\prime}\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}V_{2}^{\prime}
.

Putting the above facts together, by anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

But by forward reduction, it suffices to show that

(E[V_{1}],E[V_{2}])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}
.

For this, it suffices (by the congruence lemmas) that
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are related,
which is true by assumption.

•

Let
k\leq j
and let

\varepsilon@c^{r}\leadsto d^{r}\in\sigma_{2}
be an effect caught by

\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle\bullet
.
Let
V^{l},V^{r},E^{l}\#\varepsilon,E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be as in the statement of
Lemma
D.18
.
We need to show

(E[\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})]],\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E[E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})]])\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

If
\varepsilon\notin\sigma_{1}
, then, by the operational semantics,
both terms will step to
\mho
.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

(\mho,\mho)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}
.
This follows by ErrBot (Lemma
D.47
).

Now suppose
\varepsilon@c^{l}\leadsto d^{l}\in\sigma_{1}
.
According to the operational semantics, we have

E[\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})]]\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}E[E^{l}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{l})]],

and

\langle{\sigma_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma_{2}}\rangle E[E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})]]\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}E[E^{r}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{r})]].

Thus, by anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E[E^{l}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{l})]],

\displaystyle E[E^{r}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{r})]])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime l}
be the value to which
\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{l}
steps, and similarly
let
V^{\prime r}
be the value to which
\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{r}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle E[E^{l}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime l})]],

\displaystyle E[E^{r}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime r})]])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

As neither term steps, it is sufficient to show that they are related in

\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}
.
We assert the second disjunct in the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\sim}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket{}
,
taking
E^{l}=E[E^{l}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\bullet]]
and
E^{r}=E[E^{r}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle\bullet]]
.

We first need to show that
(V^{\prime l},V^{\prime r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket)_{k}
.
By forward reduction, it suffices to show that

(\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{l},\langle{c^{l}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{c^{r}}\rangle V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c^{r}\rrbracket).

By monotonicity of casts (lemma
D.65
), it suffices to show

(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c^{r}\rrbracket)
.
This follows from our assumption about
V^{l}
and
V^{r}
.

We now need to show that

(x^{l}.E[E^{l}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle x^{l}]],x^{r}.E[E^{r}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle x^{r}]])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket d\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}).

To this end, let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{)}\llbracket d^{l}\rrbracket_{k^{\prime}}
.
We need to show

(E[E^{l}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{1}]],E[E^{r}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{2}]])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

It will suffice by the soundness of the congruence rules to show that

(E^{l}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{1}],E^{r}[\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{2}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

Let
V_{1}^{\prime}
and
V_{2}^{\prime}
be the values to which
\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{1}
and

\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{2}
step, respectively. By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}],E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

By assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
, it suffices to show that

(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket d^{r}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
.
By forward reduction, it suffices to show

(\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{1},\langle{d^{r}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{d^{l}}\rangle V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

By monotonicity of casts (lemma
D.65
), it suffices to show

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma_{1}\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket).

This follows from our assumption on
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

∎

Corollary D.63 (commutativity of casts).

Value casts commute with effect casts.

Proof.

This follows from
D.62
, because

\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\bullet
and
\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle\bullet

satisfy the requirements in the lemma.
∎

Lemma D.64 (functoriality of casts).

Let
M
be a term such that
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\,\,|\,\,{\cdot}\vdash_{\sigma}{M}:{A}
.
Let
c:A\sqsubseteq B
and
e:B\sqsubseteq C
.
Let
d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
and let
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}

Suppose
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}M\equiv N:A
.
Then the following hold:

Identity properties:

Suppose
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}N:A
. We have

(1)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M\equiv N:A

(2)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A}\rangle M\equiv N:A

(3)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\equiv N:A

(4)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\equiv N:A

Composition properties:

Let
c:A\sqsubseteq B
and
e:B\sqsubseteq C
.
Let
d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
and
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.
Suppose
M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}N
. Then

(1)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N:C

(2)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C}\rangle M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C}\rangle N:A

(3)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle N:A

(4)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle M\mathrel{\sqsupseteq\sqsubseteq}\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle N:A

Proof.

We prove more general, “pointwise" versions of the above statements.
For instance, we show that if
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
,
then
(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.

Additionally, we only prove one direction of each of the equivalences (i.e.,
\sqsubseteq
);
the proof of the other direction is symmetric.

The statements are proven simultaneously by induction on
A
and
\sigma
.

•

Identity properties:

(1)

We need to show
(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), with
E_{1}=\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\bullet
and
E_{2}=\bullet
,
it will suffice to show the following: Let
k\leq j
and
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We will show

(\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

We continue by induction on
A
. If
A=\texttt{bool}
, then we need to show

(\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}
,
which follows from our assumption on
(V_{1},V_{2})
.

If
A=A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}
, we need to show

(\langle{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket}.

As both terms are values, it suffices to show they are related in

\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket
. So, let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let

(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket
. We need to show

((\langle{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{1})\,V^{l},V_{2}\,V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

(\langle{A_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{A}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),V_{2}\,V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By the induction hypothesis (applied twice), it suffices to show

((V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),V_{2}\,V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By the soudness of function application, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket
and

(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{i}}\rangle V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}
.
The former is true by assumption and downward closure (
k^{\prime}\leq k
).
The latter is true by inductive hypothesis, since
V^{l}
and
V^{r}
are related.

(2)

This is dual to the above.

(3)

We prove this statement by Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
).
That is, assume for all
(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket})
, we have

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket})
.
Let
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
We need to show
(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), with
E_{1}=\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle\bullet
and
E_{2}=\bullet
,
it will suffice to consider the following cases.

–

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We need to show

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

Per the operational semantics, we have
\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle V_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}V_{1}
, so by anti-reduction it
suffices to show
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
, which follows by the
assumption that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

–

Let
k\leq j
and let

\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}
be an effect caught by

\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle\bullet
– i.e.,
\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in\sigma
.
Note that, as
\sigma
is a reflexivity derivation,
c_{\varepsilon}
and
d_{\varepsilon}
are
also reflexivity derivations, i.e.,
c_{\varepsilon}^{l}=c_{\varepsilon}^{r}

and likewise for
d_{\varepsilon}
. For simplicity, let
C=c_{\varepsilon}^{l}

and
D=d_{\varepsilon}^{l}
.

Let
V^{l},V^{r},E^{l}\#\varepsilon,E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be as in the statement of
Lemma
D.18
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

According to the operational semantics, we have

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})]\mathrel{\mapsto^{1}}

\displaystyle\quad\quad\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C}\rangle V^{l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x]

So, by anti-reduction it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C}\rangle V^{l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime l}
be the term to which
\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C}\rangle V^{l}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

The above terms do not step, so it suffices to show that they are related in

\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
To this end, we will first show that

(V^{\prime l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket)_{k}
.
By forward reduction, it suffices to show that

(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C}\rangle V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket\rrbracket)_{k}
.
By the induction hypothesis, it suffices to show that
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket)_{(}k)
.

Now we will show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle x^{l}.(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle x^{l}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x]),\,x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket D\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

Let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
. We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle V_{1}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x]),\,E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

Let
V_{1}^{\prime}
be the value to which
\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle V_{1}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=V_{1}^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x]),\,E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}),

and then since the let term steps, it suffices by anti-reduction again to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}]},\,E^{r}[V_{2}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}),

By the Löb induction hypothesis, it suffices to show that

(E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}],E^{r}[V_{2}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket})

By our assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
, it suffices to show

(V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}.

By forward reduction, it suffices to show

(\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D}\rangle V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}.

By the induction hypothesis for value types, it suffices to show

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}.

This follows by assumption.

(4)

We again use Löb induction and monadic bind.

That is, assume for all
(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket})
, we have

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma}\rangle M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket})
. We need to show

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma}\rangle M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}

where
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
We again use monadic bind, and as in the previous proof, the case of related values
follows trivially since effect casts are the identity on values.
Thus, it will suffice to show the related raises case.
That is, let
k\leq j
and let

\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}
be an effect caught by

\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma}\rangle\bullet
– i.e.,
\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in\sigma
.
As in the previous proof, since
\sigma
is a reflexivity derivation,
c_{\varepsilon}
and
d_{\varepsilon}
are
also reflexivity derivations, so for simplicity, let
C=c_{\varepsilon}^{l}=c_{\varepsilon}^{r}

and
D=d_{\varepsilon}^{l}=d_{\varepsilon}^{r}
.

Let
V^{l},V^{r},E^{l}\#\varepsilon,E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be as in the statement of
the monadic bind lemma.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Note that, since
\varepsilon\in\sigma
, the downcast cannot fail.

The remainder of the proof proceeds exactly like the previous proof,
with upcasts and downcasts interchanged.

•

Composition properties:

(1)

We need to show

(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket}
.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
) with
E_{1}=\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\bullet
and
E_{2}=\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\bullet
,
it will suffice to show the following: Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.
We will show

(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{1},\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B}\rangle\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket}.

If
c\circ e=\texttt{bool}
, then
c=e=\texttt{bool}
, and we need to show

(\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle V_{1},\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket
,
which follows from our assumption.

Now suppose
c\circ e=(c_{i}\circ e_{i})\to_{(c_{\sigma}\circ e_{\sigma})}(c_{o}\circ e_{o})
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{(C_{i}\to_{\sigma_{C}}C_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{1},

\displaystyle\langle{(C_{i}\to_{\sigma_{C}}C_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\rangle\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{2})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{i}\to_{\sigma_{C}}C_{o}\rrbracket}.

Both terms are values, so it suffices to show that they are related in

\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket C_{i}\to_{\sigma_{C}}C_{o}\rrbracket
.
Let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket
.
We need to show that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\langle{(C_{i}\to_{\sigma_{C}}C_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{1})\,V^{l},

\displaystyle(\langle{(C_{i}\to_{\sigma_{C}}C_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\rangle\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{2})\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad((\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{2})\,\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime r}
be the value to which
\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{r}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad((\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle V_{2})\,V^{\prime r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction again, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

We will appeal to transitivity (Lemma
D.66
).
We continue by cases on
\sim
.

–

First suppose
\sim\,=\,<
. We first claim that

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

By the induction hypothesis applied twice, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By soundness of function application, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket
and that

(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l},\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}.

The former holds by assumption and downward closure. To show the latter, it suffices by
forward reduction to show that

(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l},\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle\langle{B_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}.

Now, by the induction hypothesis, it suffices to show that

(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{i}\rrbracket},

which follows from our assumption.

Now by transitivity, it will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})),

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

By reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
), we have
that
\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})

is related to itself.
Then by commutativity of casts (Corollary
D.63
), we can interchange the order of

\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle
and
\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle
, and the resulting terms are related.
Finally by monotonicity of casts (Lemma
D.65
), we can apply
\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle
,
and the resulting terms are still related. Moreover, all of these relations hold “at
\omega"
.

–

Now suppose
\sim\,=\,>
. By similar reasoning as in the previous case, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

Thus, by transitivity it will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l})),

\displaystyle\langle{C_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{B_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{C}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{B}}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad\quad(\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{\prime r})))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{C}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C_{o}\rrbracket}.

The reasoning is analogous to that of the previous case.

(2)

This is dual to the above.

(3)

We prove this statement by Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
).
That is, assume for all
(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket})
, we have

(\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle N)\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

Let
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
We need to show

(\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), with

E_{1}=\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle\bullet

and
E_{2}=\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle\bullet
,
it suffices to consider the follwing cases:

–

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We need to show that

(\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle V_{1},\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Since the effect cast is the identity on values, the above follows immediately
by anti-reduction.

–

Let
k\leq j
and let

\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in\sigma
be an effect caught by either
E_{1}
or
E_{2}
.
Note that, as
\sigma
is a reflexivity derivation,
c_{\varepsilon}
and
d_{\varepsilon}
are
also reflexivity derivations, i.e.,
c_{\varepsilon}^{l}=c_{\varepsilon}^{r}

and likewise for
d_{\varepsilon}
. For simplicity, let
C^{L}=c_{\varepsilon}^{l}

and
D^{L}=d_{\varepsilon}^{l}
.

Let
V^{l},V^{r},E^{l}\#\varepsilon,E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be as in the statement of
the monadic bind lemma.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Let
C^{M}
and
D^{M}
be the types such that
\varepsilon@C^{M}\leadsto D^{M}\in\sigma^{\prime}

Let
C^{R}
and
D^{R}
be the types such that
\varepsilon@C^{R}\leadsto D^{R}\in\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{L}}\rangle V^{l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{L}}\rangle V^{r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[x]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime l}
be the value to which
\langle{C^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{L}}\rangle V^{l}
steps, say in
i
steps.
Let
V^{\prime r}
be the value to which
\langle{C^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{L}}\rangle V^{r}
steps, say in
j
steps.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[x]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Now (taking
E^{\prime}=\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle\bullet\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[x]

in the
EffUpCast
rule), it will suffice by anti-reduction to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{D^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{M}}\rangle V^{\prime r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,

\displaystyle\quad\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle y\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[x]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Let
V^{\prime\prime r}
be the value to which
\langle{C^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{M}}\rangle V^{\prime r}
steps.
By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{D^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{\prime\prime r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,

\displaystyle\quad\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle y\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[x]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

As neither term steps, we will show that they belong to
\mathcal{R}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
We first need to show that

(V^{\prime l},V^{\prime\prime r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k}.

By forward-reduction, it suffices to show that

(\langle{C^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{L}}\rangle V^{l},\langle{C^{R}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{M}}\rangle\langle{C^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{C^{L}}\rangle V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k}.

By the induction hypothesis for value types, it suffices to show that

(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k}
, which is true by assumption.

Now we need to show that, for all
k^{\prime}\leq k
and related values

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle V_{1}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,y=\langle{D^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle V_{2}\,\texttt{in}\,\,

\displaystyle\quad\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle y\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[x]))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

Let
V_{1}^{\prime}
and
V_{2}^{\prime}
be the values to which
\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle V_{1}

and
\langle{D^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle V_{2}
step, respectively. By anti-reduction, it
will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}]},

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle(\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle V_{2}^{\prime}\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[x])})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

Let
V_{2}^{\prime\prime}
be the value to which
\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle V_{2}^{\prime}
steps. By anti-reduction,
it will suffice to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}]},

\displaystyle{\langle{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle({\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime\prime}]})})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

Now by the Löb induction hypothesis, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{E^{l}[V_{1}^{\prime}]},{E^{r}[V_{2}^{\prime\prime}]})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

By assumption on
E^{l}
and
E^{r}
, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle V_{1}^{\prime},V_{2}^{\prime\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)_{k^{\prime}}.

Now by forward reduction it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle V_{1},\langle{D^{L}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{M}}\rangle\langle{D^{M}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{D^{R}}\rangle V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime\prime}\rrbracket{})_{k^{\prime}}(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket).

This follows by the inductive hypothesis for value types and our assumption on

V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.

(4)

This is dual to the above: we use Löb induction and monadic bind,
and we reach a point where we need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime\prime}}\rangle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

where
\varepsilon@C^{R}\leadsto D^{R}\in\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.

If
\varepsilon\notin\sigma
, then the left-hand side steps to
\mho
, as does the right-hand side.
By ErrBot (Lemma
D.47
),
\mho
is related to itself, so by anti-reduction, we are finished.
If
\varepsilon\notin\sigma^{\prime}
, then in fact,
\varepsilon\notin\sigma
(since
\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
),
and so again, both sides step to
\mho
.

Otherwise, we proceed as in the proof of the previous case, with
the upcasts and downcasts interchanged.

∎

Lemma D.65 (monotonicity of casts).

Let
c:A\sqsubseteq B
, and
d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
, and let
M
and
N

be terms such that
\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}M\sqsubseteq N:A
.
The following hold:

(1)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N:B

(2)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{A}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B}\rangle N:A

(3)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma^{\prime}}\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma^{\prime}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma}\rangle N:A

(4)

\Sigma\,\,|\,\,\Gamma^{\sqsubseteq}\vDash_{\sigma}\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle M\sqsubseteq\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N:A

Proof.

As in the proof of the functoriality properties of casts, we prove
stronger, “pointwise” versions of the above statements, i.e., we assume

(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
, and show, for example, that

(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}
.

The proof is by induction on
c
and
d_{\sigma}
.

(1)

We need to show

(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle M,\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), with
E_{1}=E_{2}=\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle\bullet
,
it will suffice to show that

(\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{V_{1}},\langle{B}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A}\rangle{V_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B\rrbracket},

where
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

If
c=\texttt{bool}
, then we need to show
