(\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle{V_{1}},\langle{\texttt{bool}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\texttt{bool}}\rangle{V_{2}})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show that

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\texttt{bool}\rrbracket}
,
which follows from our assumption.

If
c=c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}
, then we need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle{V_{1}},\,\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle{V_{2}})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o}\rrbracket}.

As both terms are values, it suffices to show that they are related in

\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o}\rrbracket
.
Let
k^{\prime}\leq k
and let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{K}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket B_{i}\rrbracket
.
We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle(\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle{V_{1}})\,V^{l},

\displaystyle(\langle{(B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o})}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{(A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o})}\rangle{V_{2}})\,V^{r})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{B}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{o}\rrbracket}.

By anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),

\displaystyle\langle{B_{o}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{A_{o}}\rangle\langle{\sigma_{B}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{\sigma_{A}}\rangle(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{r}))

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{B}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{o}\rrbracket}.

By the inductive hypothesis applied twice, it suffices to show

((V_{1}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l}),(V_{2}\,\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{r}))\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

By soundness of function application, it suffices to show that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket

and that
(\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{l},\langle{A_{i}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{B_{i}}\rangle V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket}
.
The former is true by our assumption about
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
.
To show the latter, it suffices by the inductive hypothesis to show that

(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k^{\prime}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket B_{i}\rrbracket}
, which follows by our assumption.

(2)

This is dual to the above.

(3)

This is dual to the below, and in fact easier since these are upcasts.

(4)

We prove this statement by Löb induction (Lemma
D.16
).
That is, assume for all

(M^{\prime},N^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket})
, we have

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle M,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N)\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{j}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

Let
(M,N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
We need to show

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle M,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle N)\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

By monadic bind (Lemma
D.18
), it will suffice to consider
the following two cases:

•

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket A\rrbracket
. We need to show that

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle V_{1},\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle V_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{j}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

Since the effect cast is the identity on values, the above follows immediately
by anti-reduction.

•

Let
k\leq j
and let

\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in\sigma^{\prime}
be an effect caught by

\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\bullet
. Recalling that
\sigma^{\prime}
is shorthand for the
reflexivity derivation
\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
, we have that
c_{\varepsilon}
and
d_{\varepsilon}

are themselves reflexivity (type precision) derivations; for brevity, we
refer to the types as
C
and
D
.

Let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket C\rrbracket)_{k}
and
and let
E^{l}\#\varepsilon,E^{r}\#\varepsilon
be such that

(x^{l}.E^{l}[x^{l}],x^{r}.E^{r}[x^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\sim}\llbracket D\rrbracket)_{k}(\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

We need to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{l}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{l})],

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V^{r})])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

First, if
\varepsilon\notin\sigma
, then both sides step to
\mho
, and we are finished
by anti-reduction since
\mho
is related to itself by ErrBot (Lemma
D.47
).

Otherwise, by anti-reduction, it suffices to show

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C}\rangle V^{l})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{l}[x],

\displaystyle\texttt{let}\,\,x=\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C}\rangle V^{r})\,\texttt{in}\,\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[x])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}{(\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)}.

By the soundness of the term precision congruence rule for let, it suffices to show that (1)

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle{\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C}\rangle V^{l})},

\displaystyle{\langle{D}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{-30.0}{$\leftarrowtail$}}{D}\rangle\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(\langle{C}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{C}\rangle V^{r})})

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\sim}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{k}({\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}).

and (2) for all related
(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket)
, we have

\displaystyle(

\displaystyle\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{l}[V_{1}],\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle E^{r}[V_{2}])

\displaystyle\quad\quad\in\mathcal{E}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket A\rrbracket}.

∎

D.1.5.
Transitivity

We introduce the following notation. We define
(M_{1},M_{2})\in R_{\omega}
to mean that

(M_{1},M_{2})\in R_{k}
for all natural numbers
k
.

We now state and prove a “mixed transitivity” lemma, in which we allow
one of the two relations in the assumption to occur at a “proper" precision derivation,
while the other is constrained to occur at a reflexivity derivation.

Lemma D.66 (mixed transitivity, terms).

If (1)
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
and
(2)
(M_{2},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
,
then
(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Similarly, if
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
and

(M_{2},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
,
then
(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Proof.

This is proved simultaneuously with the following two lemmas on transitivity
for results and values.
We prove the lemma for
\sim=>
; the other case is similar.

The proof is by Löb-induction (Lemma
D.16
).
That is, assume that for all
M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime}
, and
M_{3}^{\prime}
,
if
(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{2}^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket)_{\omega}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket)
and

(M_{2}^{\prime},M_{3}^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket)_{j}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket)
,
then
(M_{1}^{\prime},M_{3}^{\prime})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket)_{j}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket)
.

We proceed by considering cases on the assumption that

(M_{2},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

In the first case,
M_{3}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j+1}}
. Then we immediately have that

(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
, via the first disjunct.

In the second case, there is
k\leq j
such that
M_{3}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}\mho

and
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{s}}\mho
, for some number of steps
s
.
By assumption (1), we have that
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{s}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
By inversion, we see that the second disjunct must have been
true (with
k=0
). This means in particular that
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
.
Thus, we may conclude using the second disjunct that

(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

In the third case, there is
k\leq j
and
N_{3}
such that
M_{3}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}N_{3}
,
and
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{s}}\mho
, for some number of steps
s
.
By similar reasoning to the previous case, we may conclude using the third
disjunct that

(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Finally, in the fourth case, there exist
k\leq j
and

(N_{2},N_{3})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}

such that
M_{2}\mathrel{\mapsto^{s}}N_{2}
for some
s
, and
M_{3}\mathrel{\mapsto^{j-k}}N_{3}
.
By assumption (1), we have that
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{s+i}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}

for all
i\in\mathbb{N}
.
By inversion, we see that either the third or the fourth disjunct was true,
with
k=i
in both cases (notice that
(s+i)-i=s
,
which is precisely the number of steps that
M_{2}
takes to
N_{2}
).

In the former case, we have
M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}\mho
and we can then finish by
asserting the third disjunct. In the latter case, there exists
N_{1}
such that

M_{1}\mathrel{\mapsto^{*}}N_{1}
and
(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{i}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
Since
i
is arbitrary, this tells us that

(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
.
To recap, we have

(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
,
and
(N_{2},N_{3})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
, for some
k\leq j
.
We want to show that
(N_{1},N_{3})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

This follows from Lemma
D.68
.

∎

Lemma D.67 (mixed transitivity, values).

If
(V_{1},V_{2})\in{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
and

(V_{2},V_{3})\in{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
,
then
(V_{1},V_{3}){\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Similarly, if
(V_{1},V_{2})\in{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
and

(V_{2},V_{3})\in{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{\omega}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
,
then
(V_{1},V_{3}){\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Proof.

Proved simultaneously with the homogeneous transitivity for terms
(Lemma
D.66
) and for results (Lemma

D.68
).
The proof is by induction on the type precision derivation
c
.
We prove the first statement only; the other is proved similarly.

•

Case
c=\texttt{bool}
. Then we have
V_{1}=V_{2}=V_{3}
and either all are

true
, or all are
false
. In either case,
V_{1}
is related to
V_{3}
.

•

Case
c=c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}
. Then
A=A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}

and
B=B_{i}\to_{\sigma_{B}}B_{o}
.

We have

(V_{1},V_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{\omega}\llbracket A_{i}\to_{\sigma_{A}}A_{o}\rrbracket
and

(V_{2},V_{3})\in\mathcal{V}^{\sim}_{k}\llbracket c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}\rrbracket
.

We need to show

(V_{1},V_{3})\in{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket c_{i}\to_{c_{\sigma}}c_{o}\rrbracket}.

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket c_{i}\rrbracket
.
We need to show that

(V_{1}\,V^{l},V_{3}\,V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket c_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c_{o}\rrbracket}.

By reflexivity (
D.30
), we know that

(V^{l},V^{l})\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket A_{i}\rrbracket
.

From our assumption about
(V_{1},V_{2})
, it follows that

(V_{1}\,V^{l},V_{2}\,V^{l})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma_{A}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A_{o}\rrbracket}.

From our assumption about
(V_{2},V_{3})
, we have

(V_{2}\,V^{l},V_{3}\,V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket c_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c_{o}\rrbracket}.

Now we apply the induction hypothesis (Lemma
D.66
)
to conclude that

(V_{1}\,V^{l},V_{3}\,V^{r})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{k}\llbracket c_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c_{o}\rrbracket},

as needed.

∎

Lemma D.68 (mixed transitivity, results).

If (1)
(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
and
(2)
(N_{2},N_{3})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
,
then
(N_{1},N_{3})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Similarly, if
(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
and

(N_{2},N_{3})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket}
,
then
(N_{1},N_{3})\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
.

Proof.

We prove only the first statement; the second is analogous.

Let
j
be fixed. We consider cases on assumption (1).
There are two subcases to consider. First,
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
are values
and
(N_{1},N_{2})\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.
Then
N_{3}
is also a value, and
(N_{2},N_{3})\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket c\rrbracket
.
By
D.67
, we have that
(N_{1},N_{3})\in\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket A\rrbracket
.

Otherwise, there exist
\varepsilon@C\leadsto D\in\sigma
,
E_{1}\#epsilon
and

E_{2}\#\varepsilon
, and
V_{1}
and
V_{2}
such that

(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket C\rrbracket)_{\omega}
, and

(x_{1}.E_{1}[x_{1}],x_{2}.E_{2}[x_{2}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket D\rrbracket{})_{\omega}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket})
, and

N_{1}=E_{1}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{1})],

and

N_{2}=E_{2}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{2})].

Similarly, since
N_{2}
and
N_{3}
are related in
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
,
it follows that
\varepsilon@c_{\varepsilon}\leadsto d_{\varepsilon}\in d_{\sigma}
, where

c_{\varepsilon}:C\sqsubseteq C^{\prime}
and
d_{\varepsilon}:D\sqsubseteq D^{\prime}
.
We also know that there exist
E_{3}\#\varepsilon
and
V_{3}
such that

(V_{2},V_{3})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{j}
, and

(x_{2}.E_{2}[x_{2}],x_{3}.E_{3}[x_{3}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket{})_{j}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket})
, and

N_{3}=E_{3}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{3})].

Recall that we need to show

(E_{1}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{1})],E_{3}[\texttt{raise}\,\,\varepsilon(V_{3})])\in\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}.

We assert the second disjunct in the definition of
\mathcal{R}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket\cdot\rrbracket{}
.

We first claim that
(V_{1},V_{3})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{j}
.
By transitivity for values (Lemma
D.67
), it suffices to show
that
(V_{1},V_{2})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{\omega}
and

(V_{2},V_{3})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{j}
. These follow by assumption.

Now we claim that

(x_{1}.E_{1}[x_{1}],x_{3}.E_{3}[x_{3}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{K}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket{})_{j}(\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}).

Let
k\leq j
and let
(V^{l},V^{r})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\varepsilon}\rrbracket)_{k}
. We need to show

(E_{1}[V^{l}],E_{3}[V^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket).

By the induction hypothesis (recall we are proving this simultaneously with transitivity
for terms, which is being proven by Löb induction), it suffices to find a term
M
such that

(E_{1}[V^{l}],M)\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{\omega}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket)
, and

(M,E_{3}[V_{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket)
.

By reflexivity (Corollary
D.30
), we have

(V^{l},V^{l})\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{V}^{\sim}\llbracket\rrbracket)_{\omega}
.

Then by our assumption on
(E_{1},E_{2})
, we have

(E_{1}[V^{l}],E_{2}[V^{l}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{})_{\omega}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A\rrbracket)

By our assumption on
(E_{2},E_{3})
we have

(E_{2}[V^{l}],E_{3}[V^{r}])\in({\blacktriangleright}\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{})_{k}(\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket),

which finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma D.69 (heterogeneous transitivity).

Let
c:A_{1}\sqsubseteq A_{2}
and
e:A_{2}\sqsubseteq A_{3}
.
Let
d_{\sigma}:\sigma\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime}
and let
d_{\sigma}^{\prime}:\sigma^{\prime}\sqsubseteq\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.

If (1)
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
and
(2)
(M_{2},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket e\rrbracket}
,
then
(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\circ e\rrbracket}
.

Similarly, if
(M_{1},M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket c\rrbracket}
and

(M_{2},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{\omega}\llbracket d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket e\rrbracket}
,
then
(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\preceq}}\llbracket c\circ e\rrbracket}
.

Proof.

Follows from mixed transitivity (Lemma
D.66
) and
the generalized cast lemmas (Lemmas

D.50
,
D.51
,

D.52
,
D.53
,

D.54
,
D.55
,

D.56
, and
D.57

).

For example, by EffDnR and ValDnR, we have

(M_{1},\,\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{2}}\rangle M_{2})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{\omega}\llbracket\sigma\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket A_{1}\rrbracket},

and by EffDnL and ValDnL, we have

(\langle{\sigma}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{\sigma^{\prime}}\rangle\langle{A_{1}}\mathrel{\rotatebox[origin]{30.0}{$\twoheadleftarrow$}}{A_{2}}\rangle M_{2},\,M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\circ e\rrbracket}.

Then applying mixed transitivity, we have

(M_{1},M_{3})\in\mathcal{E}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}_{j}\llbracket d_{\sigma}\circ d_{\sigma}^{\prime}\rrbracket{\mathcal{V}^{\mathrel{\succeq}}\llbracket c\circ e\rrbracket},

as desired.

∎
